One reviewed closure identity covers exactly those future published occurrences: each booked credit returns to its original pack once, unpaid visits close with zero credits returned, waitlists close without a debit, no fee or money movement occurs, each booking receives one in-app notice and each affected member receives one grouped email covering their canceled occurrences. Changed facts require a fresh cancellation preview and repeats do nothing. Records and provenance travel in the portable exit and native archive; the existing member-mail reconnection fence applies after restore. STUDIO-002 inherits multiple-location scope, SCHED-002 inherits appointments, SCHED-005/SCHED-013 inherit their recurring member reservations and series edits, BILL-008 inherits refunds, STAFF-001 inherits manager rights and MSG-002/005/006/007 inherit templates, preferences and additional delivery channels before shipping.

**Acceptance.**
1. Create a weekly class across DST, move one session and then the remaining series, inspect affected bookings and confirm members receive only the relevant changes.

#### SCHED-002 — Private appointments and services

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** 50Z: the current private appointment-offer foundation is native human owner only. The owner saves immutable service offers with duration, explicit start, instructor, location, room, equipment and preparation or cleanup buffers. Overlapping offers are alternatives and create no hold on the calendar. Each offer records its original availability and private conflict reasons; a later preview rechecks current allocations, instructor changes, active unavailability and applied room or location closures without overwriting the original review. Save, read and replay never run due billing, entitlement, notice or marketing work. No reservation, entitlement use, financial row or outbound message results from an offer action. Client-facing offers and acceptance are the named missing SCHED-015 gap, which inherits the original concurrent booking, deposit and reschedule acceptance and the appointment billing, cancellation, staff-door and member-door obligations below; SCHED-013 inherits recurring reservations. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Only the native human owner can create or read private offers, with refused non-owner requests beside successful owner requests; an offer with a conflict retains the reason; no offer action creates a calendar reservation, entitlement use, financial row or outbound message.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 appointment cancellation, move, restore and member rescheduling fences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
3. SCHED-005 obligation before this capability ships: weekly private-appointment reservations from the member app inherit the same per-occurrence entitlement, horizon, capacity, FIFO, studio-local DST, visible skipped-reason, cancellation, late-cancel and no-show and once-only future-series acceptance; assert booked/waitlisted/skipped appointments through the public member door at 1440 and 390 pixels.

#### SCHED-003 — Room equipment and instructor conflicts

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Scheduling blocks overlapping instructor, room, reformer or shared-equipment allocations, including preparation buffers, availability exceptions and concurrent edits; managers see the conflicting records before publication. Room and equipment names identify resources across the current studio, ignoring case and leading, trailing or repeated whitespace; equipment includes individually named reformers. Preparation and cleanup are whole minutes from 0 through 1440 inclusive before and after each class (default zero), applied to every allocated resource. Conflict checks compare half-open UTC intervals `[start − preparation, end + cleanup)`, so exact boundary contact is allowed; the studio time zone controls entry and display. The owner records, updates or removes instructor, room and equipment unavailable intervals with a reason; an exception cannot overlap an already-published allocation. Exception reasons require the owner content right. A preparation-only agent grant receives the blocking resource, interval and record ID without the reason; members and instructors receive no exception records. An authorized agent read intersects the sponsor's owner content right under company policy. Preparation and publication both recheck current records under the shared transaction fence; a blocked draft names the conflicting record, resource and occupied interval, and no part of the draft publishes. The current class-week doors conform now; future scheduling doors SCHED-001, SCHED-002, SCHED-004, SCHED-005, SCHED-007, SCHED-008, SCHED-010–014 and CHANNEL-001–003 inherit these checks before they ship.

**Acceptance.**
1. Try to publish overlapping classes using the same instructor, room or equipment, including a concurrent change; each conflict is refused and displayed without partial publication.
2. Exercise both preparation buffers, unavailable intervals and exact touching boundaries through the public commands and browser forms; a new exception blocks a prepared draft but cannot invalidate a published class.
3. Verify owner-only reasons are absent from member, instructor and agent responses, including agent preparation errors.

#### SCHED-004 — Instructor substitutions

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Instructors request coverage; managers see qualified available substitutes, approve one and update the assigned roster, pay rate and member notifications. Until approved the original assignment remains visible.

**Acceptance.**
1. Request a substitute, reject a conflicting candidate, approve an available instructor and verify their roster rights and pay report change while the original instructor loses attendance rights.
2. the native owner acts for the manager until STAFF-001.
3. An assigned instructor or owner requests coverage for a future published class; the original assignment remains until exact owner approval.
4. The owner records immutable versions of studio approval for an active instructor and exact trimmed service name; this is an operational qualification, never a certification claim.
5. Candidates show qualification and current scheduling conflicts, including room/equipment allocations, preparation/cleanup buffers and availability exceptions.
6. A preview requires a different qualified available instructor and lists the before/after assignment, substitute pay rule/version or honestly unavailable rate, unchanged credits/money and every exact member notice.
7. Qualification, source request, class, booking, availability, person, pay-rule or mail-connection changes invalidate it.
8. Approval rechecks allocations and future start under the transaction fence, transfers authoritative roster/check-in rights, retains original/current instructor and owner/time provenance and appends substitution history. STAFF-002 selects the approved substitute rate for the new instructor; the old instructor loses the class and earned compensation. Every affected active booking receives one in-app notice and one configured Base email reserved after commit with honest provider outcome; request reasons remain in the coverage record rather than being added to member notices. Exact request, qualification and approval replay never repeats an effect or notice; a changed request or preview refuses. Private requests and qualification management follow existing owner/assigned-instructor doors; members and preparation-only agent grants have no coverage records or approval authority. Portable exit and native restore retain qualifications, requests, previews, history and once-only identities, with existing restored-mail reconnection fences. SCHED-002 inherits appointment substitution, STAFF-001 inherits manager permissions and denial/revocation cases, and MSG-002 inherits notification template/preferences before those capabilities ship.

#### SCHED-005 — Recurring member reservations

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Members reserve recurring classes or appointment slots within their entitlement and booking horizon, with explicit exceptions for full, closed or ineligible sessions; each occurrence has its own cancellation and waitlist outcome. The member chooses a published timetable occurrence and an inclusive end date within that timetable; no unpublished future slot is invented. Each occurrence is judged at its own start against its own usable credit or plan allowance, service, expiry, waiver, booking window, capacity and FIFO waitlist. Full classes waitlist without spending a credit; closed, already reserved or ineligible dates are explicitly skipped with a retained reason the member reads. A proposal that would exceed available credit/plan visits or the booking horizon is refused atomically. Review names every date and confirmation rechecks the facts. Occurrence instants use the timetable’s studio-local clock across DST and retain each booking’s ordinary late-cancel/no-show terms. Future-series cancellation previews each effect, releases only live unstarted occurrences once, follows a moved booking’s identity and never changes past, started or already canceled visits. Appointment-reservation scope: SCHED-002 inherits weekly member private-appointment reservations with the same per-occurrence acceptance before shipping, and SCHED-013/STAFF-001 inherit staff-created appointment series and delegated staff booking-right grant, denial and revocation cases before shipping. Until STAFF-001 the native human owner acts for staff holding the booking right.

**Acceptance.**
1. Reserve a weekly class across a studio-local DST change with one full occurrence, one holiday closure and one ineligible occurrence; inspect booked/waitlisted/skipped dates and retained reasons, and prove an earlier waiter is promoted first.
2. Cancel one occurrence, move another through the ordinary booking door, then preview and cancel the future series twice: each future reservation is released once, credits return only to their own packs under their own cancellation terms, and past or started visits are unchanged.
3. Apply late-cancel and no-show fees per occurrence, restore through the ordinary staff door and retain series provenance.
4. Refuse a series past the booking horizon, a series over credit or plan-visit limits, a stale confirmation and an unlisted staff actor without writes.
5. Through the member and current owner booking-right doors at 1440 and 390 pixels, review and confirm the same outcomes and cancellation effects; retain actor, channel and before/after in one canonical staff action per command with linked per-occurrence cancellation history.
6. Native export/import preserves the series, previews, reasons, booking identities and credit history.

#### SCHED-006 — Booking windows and cancellation rules

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Owners configure opening and closing windows, member priority, cutoff times and cancellation policies by service or plan. Online-bookable capacity is set separately from total capacity under SCHED-011, and permissioned staff overrides of windows are SCHED-010. Members see the applicable rule before booking and the exact charge or credit effect before cancellation. Staff canceling or moving a booking for a member see the same effect before confirming and choose a late cancel under policy or an early cancel that waives it, under SCHED-014.

**Acceptance.**
1. Book at both sides of a booking-window boundary and cancel before and after a service cutoff; the displayed policy, credit return and fee match persisted outcomes.
2. As front desk, cancel a member's booking after the cutoff and see the same fee in the staff preview before choosing late or early cancel.
3. a service's rule replaces the studio rule, a plan's own rule (PLAN-002) replaces both for the bookings that plan pays for, and with none booking stays open until start with every pre-start cancellation early.
4. Hours and minutes are elapsed time before the class's UTC start (a DST change moves the wall-clock boundary); booking is allowed from the opening instant inclusive until the closing instant exclusive, checked again at commit on the database clock; a cancellation at or after the cutoff instant is late.
5. Member priority is a pack sold with priority booking: inside the priority-only window only such a pack can seat the member.
6. Each booking keeps the cancellation terms in force when it was made (cutoff, credit effect, fee), so a later rule change never worsens it; bookings made before SCHED-006 keep the original free-cancellation rule.
7. A late cancel either keeps the spent credit (recorded as consumed) or returns it, and adds the fee to the member's balance as one charge; Strap shows that as a fee added, never as a card charge or a collected payment, which ATT-002 and BILL-004 carry.
8. Leaving a waitlist is always free. The shown effect carries a terms key; a cancel sent with a key that no longer matches is refused with no writes. members cannot waive and instructors and preparation-only agent grants cannot cancel for members. Windows apply to owner desk bookings too; the audited override is SCHED-010's. Future doors inherit these rules before they ship: SCHED-010 (window override), SCHED-014 (moves, staff reasons, notices, cancellations list), ATT-002 (fee authorization, pending versus collected, collection), PLAN-001/PLAN-002 (plan-level rules and priority for memberships), MSG-004 (late-window promotion stop), and SCHED-002, SCHED-005, SCHED-007, SCHED-009, SCHED-012 and SCHED-013 for their bookings.

#### SCHED-007 — Spot and reformer selection

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Members select a named room spot, bike or reformer from an accessible map or list; disabled spots are unavailable, instructors see assignments and concurrent requests cannot claim the same equipment. Staff holding the booking right (STAFF-001) assign or move a member's spot from the roster on the member's behalf under the same fence, and the change is audited (SEC-002). the owner names a room's spots in room order and marks spots out of service; spot names and room names ignore case and repeated spaces. A class whose room has spots seats each booked member on exactly one in-service spot, and its seats are the fewer of its capacity and the room's in-service spots (online and desk-held pools are capped the same way). A booking names a free spot or, naming none, takes the first free spot in room order; a waitlisted booking holds no spot, and promotion seats the waiter on the first free spot. Every booking and move shares the domain revision fence, so of two concurrent claims on one spot exactly one commits and the other is refused with no writes. Members see each spot as free, taken or out of service, never who holds it; instructors and the owner see who is on each spot. A member moves their own booked seat, and instructors, other members and agents holding only preparation grants cannot. Each move is kept on the booking (from, to, actor, role, time) and a staff move is marked staff-made with the right and channel used. A spot held by a booked seat in an upcoming class cannot be removed or taken out of service until it is moved. Future doors inherit this before they ship: SEC-002 (search and export of these move records), STAFF-001 (front desk's own booking right), SCHED-014 (class moves keep a spot under this fence), SCHED-002, SCHED-005, SCHED-012 and SCHED-013 (their bookings take spots), RPT-002 (out-of-service spots in denominators), and STUDIO-002 (location-scoped rooms).

**Acceptance.**
1. On mobile select a reformer, race another member for that spot, move to a free spot and inspect the instructor roster; only one booking owns each spot.
2. Front desk moves a booked member to another free reformer from the roster; the instructor roster updates once and the move is audited as staff-made.

#### SCHED-008 — Closures and class cancellation

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Managers close a room or location or cancel a class with an impact preview, reason, approved credit/refund handling and delivered notifications; waitlists close and future recurrence follows the chosen scope. Mail configuration is described in [Studio mechanisms](docs/contract/MECHANISMS.md#sched-008-mail-configuration).

**Acceptance.**
1. Cancel a full class and its waitlist due to a closure, confirm affected members and financial effects, submit twice and verify each credit/refund and notification occurs once.
2. instructors, members and preparation-only agent keys are refused with no writes.
3. The owner cancels one class, or closes a room (named in any letter case or spacing) or the whole location for an interval; a closure affects every published class there that has not started and whose own start and end overlap the interval [start, end), entered on the studio's clock.
4. Preparing records a preview and changes nothing else: each affected class, each booked member (their credit returns to its own pack) and waitlisted member (the waitlist spot closes, no credit used), the member, credit and waitlist totals, that no money moves, and whether email is configured.
5. Confirming requires that exact impact; any booking change since the preview refuses the confirmation until the owner previews again.
6. Confirming cancels the classes with the reason, returns each booked credit once (a pack restore event), closes each waitlist, assesses no fee, writes one in-app notice per affected booking (shown on the member's account until they dismiss it) and reserves one email per affected member per closure covering all of their cancelled bookings; a second or concurrent confirmation changes and sends nothing.
7. Cancellation email uses the configured Resend, SendGrid or Cloudflare provider and studio sender, separate from sign-in mail, only after cancellation commits; its reservation identity prevents a second send.
8. The owner sees each member's outcome exactly as recorded: accepted by the provider (delivery not confirmed), refused, rate limited, outcome unknown, interrupted, no email address, or not configured. A room closure also blocks that room for new classes in its interval (SCHED-003); a location closure blocks every new class in its interval. Cancelled classes free their instructor, room and equipment and cannot be booked. Money refunds are BILL-008's, series scope SCHED-001's, per-location scope STUDIO-002's, SMS MSG-005's, Postmark email MSG-006's and templates and preferences MSG-002's obligations (pattern 1).

#### SCHED-009 — Absence credits and makeups

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Course and youth-class policies grant absence credits only when eligible, expire them explicitly and let authorized parents or participants book capacity-safe makeups without replacing the original attendance record. Staff holding the booking right record an absence a parent or participant reports at the desk or by phone and book the makeup on their behalf, under the same eligibility and capacity rules, audited as staff-made (SEC-002). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Report an eligible absence, issue one makeup credit, book a replacement through the parent portal, repeat the request and inspect one credit and linked original/makeup attendance.
2. Front desk records a phoned-in absence and books the makeup for the parent; one credit is issued and used and the audit shows both actions as staff-made.
3. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 cancellation, move and restore for makeups; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
4. only the owner configures eligible service, minimum pre-start notice and expiry in elapsed days after the original class.
5. Each eligible original booked seat issues one makeup credit, retaining an absence on the original seat and keeping its original credit or period visit used.
6. A makeup is a different session of the same service before that credit’s explicit expiry, with ordinary waiver, booking-window, duplicate, spot and capacity fences.
7. The participant uses their own door; an owner records verified youth/legal-guardian evidence and the guardian accepts through their own sign-in. Adult delegates additionally need the participant’s acceptance. This grants only absence/makeup facts, never a household, contact, payer, health profile or general child account. The owner uses the booking staff door until STAFF-001; both phoned-in actions retain one canonical actor/member/right/channel/before/after record. Cancellation returns an eligible makeup credit once with its original expiry, move/restore reuse the same credit and source link, and full-target move offers keep the original seat until a valid claim. Initial full-class makeups refuse without reserving a credit. PLAN-009, MEM-002, STAFF-001, SEC-003 and MOVE-009 inherit these authority, identity, retention and allocation fences before their new doors ship. A legal-guardian grant records, from owner-checked evidence, the date the participant turns 18 and ends then. A youth participant never needs a sign-in or acceptance and cannot revoke a legal-guardian grant; the guardian, or the owner with a recorded reason, revokes it. Revocation is checked at commit on every door, replay and snapshot; existing credits and bookings stay the participant's, and the revoked party receives no further notices. Absence and makeup notices also go to each accepted guardian. A grant confers no waiver-signing, household or payer authority; MEM-002's relationships replace this store when MEM-002 ships. A grant replayed with changed facts is refused. Comp, unpaid and guest seats, no-shows, late cancels and makeup bookings never earn a credit. When an early cancel would return the credit, the absence preview shows it. Before the original starts, staff may restore an absence whose credit is unused, voiding it once. A policy may cap credits per participant per rolling number of elapsed days.

#### SCHED-010 — Front-desk booking for members and walk-ins

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Permissioned front-desk staff, managers and owners book a member into a class or appointment at the desk or by phone, choose which eligible pass, credit or membership the visit uses, sell a drop-in or pack in the same checkout, or record an unpaid visit that the member's next eligible purchase settles exactly once; a phone sale with no card present is paid through a BILL-002 payment link sent by SMS or email. With the separate override right (STAFF-001) staff book past a full roster or outside the booking window; each override records the actor, reason and bypassed policy. Staff-made bookings keep the waiver, duplicate, capacity and credit fences of member bookings. A standing series for a member is booked under SCHED-013; canceling, moving or restoring a member's booking and placing a member on a waitlist are SCHED-014. instructors, members and preparation-only agent grants hold neither. A desk booking records the request channel (desk, phone or inbox), the staff actor and right, and how the visit is paid: the member's eligible credit that expires first, a pack the desk chooses (it must be the member's own, unexpired, have a credit left, cover the class's service, outlast the class start and, inside a priority-only window, carry priority booking; otherwise refused with no writes), a drop-in or pack sold in the same checkout, or an unpaid visit. A sale creates the member's pack, its charge and, when the desk enters a reference, a payment recorded as received outside Strap; Strap never charges a card here. A drop-in is one credit for this class's service that ends when the class ends. An unpaid visit holds a seat with no credit and no debit; checking it in or marking it a no-show spends nothing yet, and cancelling it releases it (any late fee in its terms still applies). The member's next purchased pack (approved or sold at the desk, never a drop-in) that covers an unpaid visit settles it once, earliest class first, with one reserve (and the consume a recorded check-in or no-show already implies); a later purchase never settles it again. Without an override, desk bookings meet the member fences: the waiver, duplicate, booking window (SCHED-006), capacity (SCHED-011 held then online pools) and credit fences, and a full class offers only the MSG-004 waitlist. With an override reason (1 to 500 characters) the owner books past a full roster or outside the booking window; the booking keeps the override (actor, role, right, reason, time and each bypassed policy, such as class full (12 of 12) or booking window opens at its instant), takes a desk-held allocation and gets a spot only when one is named and free. No override is recorded when no policy was bypassed. The member sees a desk booking as their own booking in Strap; no message is sent. Future doors inherit these obligations before they ship: BILL-002 (card checkout and the phone sale's SMS or email payment link), SALE-002 (card-present sale at the desk), MSG-002 (the booking confirmation a desk booking sends), STAFF-001 (front desk's and managers' own booking and override rights), SEC-002 (search and export of overrides and staff-made bookings), SCHED-002 (appointments booked at the desk), PLAN-001/PLAN-002 (memberships chosen as the visit's entitlement), SCHED-013 (standing series) and SCHED-014 (cancel, move and restore).

**Acceptance.**
1. As front desk, book a phoned-in member choosing between two eligible packs, sell a drop-in at the moment of booking, and book a walk-in as an unpaid visit that a later ten-pack purchase settles with one debit; attempt a full-class and an outside-window booking without the override right (refused, no writes) and with it (booked, override audited); inspect exactly one credit debit per visit throughout.

#### SCHED-011 — Online capacity and held walk-in spots

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001

**Requirement.** Each class and appointment type has a total capacity and a lower online-bookable capacity. The member app, website widget, ClassPass and Wellhub draw only from online capacity while front desk fills the held spots up to the total; waitlist promotion respects which capacity a cancellation freed. Rosters and occupancy reports show online, desk-held and booked spots. Current class-week, member booking and owner-operated desk doors conform now. no capacity override is offered by this door. A walk-in uses an active member profile, an eligible pack and their own current waiver acknowledgment. Online capacity is an integer from zero through total capacity inclusive; omitting it preserves all-online capacity for older classes. Existing bookings belong to the online pool. Desk bookings use held spots first, then spare online spots after eligible online waiters are promoted. Each confirmed booking retains its allocation pool and request channel; cancellation frees that pool, and only an available online allocation can promote an online waiter, under the same total seat and credit fence. The class schedule is the current occupancy report and shows total, online and held allocations and booked counts; members see anonymous online availability derived from all bookings. Future appointment, staff, guest, widget, aggregator and reporting doors SCHED-002, SCHED-010, SCHED-012–014, STAFF-001, APP-001, APP-002, CHANNEL-001, CHANNEL-002 and RPT-002 inherit these capacity and reporting obligations before shipping.

**Acceptance.**
1. Set a class to total 12 and online 10; fill 10 through member doors and see it full online with the waitlist offered; front desk books spot 11 for a member and spot 12 for a walk-in; a 13th staff booking without the override right is refused; a member cancellation reopens one online spot and promotes the first eligible waiter once.

#### SCHED-012 — Booking a named guest

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** From the branded app, website widget or member account a member books a seat for a named friend or family member alongside or instead of themselves, using a guest pass (PLAN-010), their own credit or a paid guest drop-in; the guest needs no account. The guest receives the waiver to sign under MEM-005 before check-in and becomes a deduplicated lead under LEAD-001 with the referring member recorded. Both seats count against the same capacity fence, and front desk sees and checks in the guest. Staff holding the booking right book a named guest for a member at the desk or by phone, drawing on that member's guest pass, credit or a paid guest drop-in under the same waiver, lead and capacity rules. studio implementation: a confirmed guest booking for an account-holding consenting guest creates a private inquiry only when none exists, assigns it to an active native owner and retains the host and source reservation. Existing inquiries keep their assignment, source, stage and original attribution; every subsequent booking retains its own source without creating another inquiry. Preview, failed commit and replay never duplicate the inquiry or booking source. Cancellation retains the source chronology. These records confer no marketing permission or referral reward and trigger no additional outbound message. Named-guest offer scope: owner-only access, immutable first attribution and no implied marketing consent, reward or extra outbound action; STAFF-001, SEC-003 and LEAD-001 inherit scoped access, used-person retention and identity matching before their new doors ship. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. A member with two guest passes books themselves and a named guest into a class with two seats left; one guest pass is consumed, the class is full, a lead exists with the member as referrer, and the guest's check-in is refused until the waiver is signed from the emailed link; a concurrent booking for the last seat is refused or waitlisted; canceling the guest seat restores the guest pass once.
2. Front desk books a phoned-in guest for the same member into another class on the remaining pass; the pass count falls once and the booking is audited as staff-made.
3. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 cancellation, move and restore for guest bookings; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
4. PLAN-010 obligation before new guest and payer doors ship: preserve guest-owned host consent (an account-holding guest authorizes the host; a guest without an account authorizes only their own seat through their own emailed link before check-in, and the host never learns whether an entered guest matches an existing account), payer-owned charge authorization, studio-wide per-guest limits on the deduplicated guest identity and the separate once-only allocation and charge ledger; STAFF-001 asserts denial and revocation at each staff door.

#### SCHED-013 — Staff-booked recurring appointment series

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Permissioned front-desk staff, managers, owners and instructors with the booking right (STAFF-001) book a standing series for a member from the staff web app or mobile (APP-004), for example a weekly private on Tuesdays at 9:00 with one instructor on reformer 2 until a set end date or occurrence count. The same staff door books a recurring class reservation for a member (SCHED-005) with the same per-occurrence booked, waitlisted and skipped outcomes. The series fixes the service, instructor, room or equipment, studio-local time and end. Before saving, every occurrence is checked against instructor availability and time-off exceptions, room and equipment conflicts and buffers (SCHED-003), studio closures, and the member's entitlement or payment (a pack session reserved per occurrence, a membership allowance or a pay-per-visit price). Conflicts are listed by date with alternative times or instructors to choose, and a chosen alternative is checked and saved as that week's occurrence, or the date is skipped explicitly; none is silently dropped. Saved occurrences hold their slots against member-app, widget and aggregator bookings. Editing or canceling one occurrence, or this and future occurrences, uses the SCHED-001 impact preview and notifies the member once per change; a canceled occurrence returns its reserved session once, and instructor pay (STAFF-002) follows each occurrence actually taught. studio semantics, 27 October 2026: native owners grant explicit, MFA-protected manager, front-desk and instructor rights bounded by named members and locations. Booking and member-record authority remain separate; an instructor can save only occurrences assigned to that instructor, private conflict reasons remain owner-only, and authority is checked before both pending confirmation and committed replay. Existing scoped grants use the canonical STAFF-001 hub. Private occurrences can reserve finite-pack sessions or membership allowances; explicit skipped dates retain their reasons and create no holds or financial rows. A separate sale right permits a published owner price to create one native pay-per-visit balance obligation per saved occurrence, with the exact price retained and no payment collection. Household pay-per-visit is refused until payer-owned authority is proved. Superseded prices require a fresh review; cancellation and restore retain the original amount and linked adjustment chronology. One or following moves are checked as a batch in original occurrence order with studio-local dates across DST. Restore reuses original class, booking and funding identities and rechecks resources, waiver, intake, window and allowance. Cancellation and move previews disclose each retained policy fee and effective fee; an early waiver after cutoff requires its separate fee-waiver right and reason. Moves carry their original allocation, cancellation returns an eligible allocation once, and restore reverses only that cancellation fee without a second debit of a retained late allocation. Each committed change has one canonical named-right staff action, one grouped member notice and one configured Base email reservation, with honest unavailable, paused and opted-out outcomes. An explicit courteous early cancellation may suppress its notice only with a retained suppression reason; late policy suppression is refused and the canonical audit records the suppression. Private teaching pay counts ended attended occurrences only. Native archive restore retains private records, canonical grants and funding chronology, excludes sessions and MFA, and rechecks fresh authority on replay. Ct approved these expanded conservative defaults with riders on 27 October 2026; STAFF-001, SCHED-005, SCHED-014 and SCHED-015 retain their original inherited obligations. Transport and record details are in [Studio mechanisms](docs/contract/MECHANISMS.md#sched-013). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. As front desk, book a ten-week Tuesday private series for a member holding a ten-session private pack; one week falls on an instructor time-off exception and is listed as a conflict before saving with alternatives.
2. Choose the offered Wednesday 9:00 slot with the same instructor for that week, save, and see ten occurrences and ten sessions reserved, including the rebooked Wednesday; a concurrent widget booking for any of those slots is refused.
3. Move one occurrence to Wednesday through the preview, then cancel this and future occurrences from week six; the pack shows exactly the sessions not used or reserved, the member receives one notice per change, and the pay report counts only the sessions taught.
4. Front desk also books a member's weekly 6:00 pm class reservation for eight weeks: one full week is waitlisted, one holiday closure is skipped and the other six are booked with one credit each.
5. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 cancellation, move and restore for recurring appointment occurrences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
6. SCHED-005 obligation before this capability ships: staff-created private-appointment series inherit every SCHED-005 per-occurrence acceptance; current owner-made recurring class reservations already use the same reviewed member outcomes and canonical booking-right audit.
7. Assert weekly appointments, alternatives, and delegated staff rights through the public staff door at 1440 and 390 pixels. studio owners, scoped managers and front-desk staff, and instructors limited to their own assigned occurrences, use explicit MFA-protected booking and member-record authority. Private resource-conflict reasons remain owner-only. Every preview, confirmation and committed replay rechecks current rights and scope. A separate sale right permits an exact currently published price to create one native balance obligation per saved occurrence, without collection or payment representation. Household priced visits remain denied until the payer’s own exact authorization is proved; this is unfinished household support, not a permanent exclusion. Cancellation, move and restore preserve original booking, funding and price identities and append linked adjustments without another debit. A late-policy waiver needs its separate current right and reason. Each committed change retains one canonical staff action and the applicable grouped notice and email reservation, with unavailable, paused, opted-out and uncertain outcomes distinguished and no automatic resend from uncertainty. Complete SCHED-005 per-occurrence and SCHED-014 change behavior and STAFF-006 paired doors remain required. SCHED-015 retains member appointment acceptance and deposits; this approval does not narrow either clause. Remaining SCHED-013 proof: complete the original concurrent widget refusal and all inherited appointment acceptance. Prove household priced-visit refusal and later payer-owned authorization, changed waiver, intake and window refusals at private restore, private no-show and cancellation-history coverage, complete delegated rights and canonical staff pairs, and private uncertain-send reconciliation without resend. Preserve original funding chronology through native restore and current-authority replay. Existing native competitor refusal does not complete an absent widget or aggregator door. Current SCHED-013 proof, 27 October 2026: the documented evidence “Private desk reservations atomically hold resources and sessions, preview moves and future releases, notify once and pay only taught sessions” now asserts private no-show consumption without taught pay and atomic restore refusal after changed waiver, service intake and booking-window facts. “Private appointment changes reserve one configured Base email and keep unavailable, paused and member-suppressed outcomes honest” now asserts an uncertain private provider outcome survives replay, reads and scheduled reconciliation without another send. the documented evidence “privatePayment” asserts household priced-visit refusal both before and after general household payer acceptance and refuses an individual-price confirmation after household membership changes; exact private-price authority remains unfinished. “privatePolicy” asserts a private cancellation’s actor, role, booking right, channel, reason, retained fee, credit effect, matching filtered CSV and XLSX and historical late count after restoration, plus the original private no-show fee after a policy change without a second allocation debit. the documented evidence “appointmentsBrowser” exercises the private cancellations list and retained restoration at 1440 and 390, in addition to the existing series screens. These assertions close only the named items in the dated Remaining proof inventory.  the documented evidence “privatePolicy” also proves manager and assigned-instructor save, move, cancel and restore with separate current grants, refused missing rights and scope, revoked pending confirmation and committed replay, original identities, once-only credits, notices and canonical events. the documented evidence “delegatedPrivateBrowser” exercises each role’s cancellation and restoration through the 390-pixel staff forms and retains their 1440-pixel screens. This bounded series proof does not certify the full STAFF-001 role matrix or replace SCHED-015 member-owned acceptance.

#### SCHED-014 — Staff cancellations, moves and restores of member bookings

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Staff holding the booking right (STAFF-001: held by owners, managers and front desk) cancel, move or restore a member's single class or appointment booking on the member's behalf from the class roster (sign-in sheet), the member profile, the member's inbox conversation (MSG-001) or a staff phone or tablet (APP-004), and place a member on a waitlist, remove them from it or accept a waitlist offer for them (MSG-004); a chosen waitlist position needs the override right. Every staff cancellation takes a studio-defined cancellation reason. Before the SCHED-006 cutoff it is an early cancel: the spent credit, membership visit or guest pass returns once (an unpaid visit is released), and the seat goes to the first eligible waiter under MSG-004, exactly as BOOK-002. After the cutoff staff see the SCHED-006 policy effect (the lost visit or the ATT-002 fee amount) before confirming and choose either a late cancel under policy, which assesses that lost visit or fee once through ATT-002, or an early cancel that waives the penalty; waiving needs the separate fee-waiver right and a reason. A move transfers a booking in one step to another eligible class session or appointment time: the same credit or entitlement carries over with no second debit; the new seat passes the waiver, entitlement, duplicate, capacity, online and desk-held capacity (SCHED-011) and spot (SCHED-007) fences; a full target puts the member on its waitlist while they keep the original seat unless staff hold the override right; and the freed seat goes to the original session's first eligible waiter once. A move made after the original session's cutoff offers the same late or early choice. The member receives one MSG-002 notice per change; staff may suppress it for a courtesy cancel, and the suppression is recorded. A booking canceled in error is restored from the cancellations list or the profile while its session has not started and a seat is free (or past a full roster with the override right): it reuses the returned credit with no second debit and reverses any fee from that cancellation once, and a seat already given to a promoted waiter is never taken back. Bulk early cancel of selected roster members processes each booking separately under its own identity, so one failure neither stops nor repeats the others. The cancellations list shows each canceled booking with member, session, cancellation time, actor (the member, the staff member and role, or the system), channel, early or late type, fee or lost-visit outcome, reason, notice state and restore state. It filters by date, class, staff member and type, feeds RPT-002 and exports through RPT-006. Every staff cancel, move, restore, waitlist action and waiver is recorded as staff-made in SEC-002. An agent cancellation, move or restore carries standard, adds money for a charge or fee waiver and external for a member notice; the company policy decides execution. Members keep their own cancel and reschedule doors (BOOK-002, SCHED-002, SCHED-006, APP-003). instructors, other members and preparation-only agent keys cannot execute staff actions. Owners manage the studio cancellation-reason list; every staff cancel or move selects one, and an early choice inside the cutoff also takes a waiver reason. A twelve-hour cutoff includes six hours before start: the preview first shows the $15 policy fee, then the explicit early/Sick waiver shows an effective $0 fee. There is no implied grace interval. A move always carries its original reserved credit, with no second debit or lost visit; a late move assesses only the disclosed monetary fee, while a reasoned early move waives it. A full target creates a pending move tied to the source booking: the source seat and credit remain, a vacancy always triggers a timed claim offer, and acceptance confirms the current source-effect key (a cutoff change requires fresh confirmation). A successful explicit move elsewhere closes the pending requests. Capacity overrides require a reason and never take another person’s spot. Restores reuse the original credit allocation (re-reserving a returned credit, not a second purchase debit), reverse only that cancellation’s fee once and never evict a promoted waiter. Current eligibility, waiver, window, capacity and spot fences are checked at commit. Each committed change has one in-app notice and one email reserved through the configured Base mail provider; unavailable, paused, provider-accepted, uncertain and suppressed outcomes remain distinct, with no mailbox-delivery claim. Only a courtesy early cancel can suppress the notice, with a retained suppression reason. Bulk early cancellations have separate identities and outcomes. The cancellations list and CSV use identical studio-local cancellation-date, class, actor and type filters; the late count counts historical late actions once even after restoration. CSV data cells use JSON values inside RFC 4180 quoted fields, as the portable exit does; headers are plain names, cents are integers and absent fields are null. Existing closure/system records are included from their retained facts, with missing historical fields explicitly labelled not recorded. Original action records retain actor, role, right, channel, reason, before/after, fee/credit effect, notice and restore identity. Agents prepare immutable cancellation or move drafts; under the held company policy the named person approves the exact unchanged facts once. MSG-001 inherits the conversation door; APP-004 the staff device doors; SCHED-002 private appointments and member rescheduling; PLAN-002 and PLAN-010 membership/guest entitlements; SCHED-005/009/012/013 their record kinds and doors; SEC-002 searchable/exportable staff action history; MSG-002/005/006/007 their templates, preferences and provider channels; and RPT-002/RPT-006 their wider reports and XLSX/scheduled exports. These future clauses must assert their inherited behavior before they ship.

**Acceptance.**
1. As front desk holding the booking right on a 390-pixel staff view, with a 12-hour cutoff and a $15 ATT-002 late-cancel fee, cancel a member's class 6 hours before start from the roster with reason "Sick": the policy preview shows the $15 fee; explicitly choose an early cancel with a Sick waiver and see the effective $0 fee; one credit returns, the first eligible waiter is promoted once and the member receives one notice.
2. Cancel a second member 2 hours before start from their profile: the preview shows the $15 fee; choose late cancel and see one fee assessed.
3. On a third member, choose early cancel with a waiver reason and suppress the notice: no fee is assessed, the credit returns, no notice is sent, and the waiver and suppression are audited.
4. Move a fourth member from the 6:00 pm class to the full 7:00 pm class: without the override right they are waitlisted for 7:00 pm and keep their 6:00 pm seat; then move them into the second 7:00 pm class in the other room, which has space: one credit debit in total, and the freed 6:00 pm seat promotes a waiter once.
5. Restore the first cancellation while a seat is open, with no extra debit; restoring into a seat already taken by a promoted waiter is refused without the override right.
6. A front-desk user without the booking right, and one without the fee-waiver right choosing early cancel after the cutoff, are refused with no writes; a preparation-only agent key is refused.
7. The cancellations list shows each action with its actor, channel, early or late type and reason, RPT-002 counts the late cancel once, and the RPT-006 export equals the list.

#### SCHED-015 — Client-facing appointment offers and acceptance

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002

**Requirement.** Members book private sessions from service duration, staff availability, location, equipment and buffers; deposits, packages, rescheduling and cancellation use the same entitlement and billing rules as classes. Staff move or cancel a member's appointment on the member's behalf under SCHED-014. Client-facing offers and acceptance remain unimplemented; SCHED-002 private owner alternatives are not an invitation, a booking, a hold or authority to charge. Every obligation elsewhere in this contract that names SCHED-002 for future appointment booking also binds SCHED-015. This clause inherits SCHED-002's original member booking, deposits, packages, rescheduling, cancellation and concurrent resource fence, SCHED-014's change previews, STAFF-006's matching staff doors and SCHED-005's per-occurrence reservation fences before those capabilities ship.

**Acceptance.**
1. Offer two overlapping appointment slots using one instructor and room, book concurrently, collect a deposit and reschedule; only one conflicting reservation commits.
2. SCHED-013 boundary: member-facing appointment acceptance and deposit authorization remain SCHED-015 obligations.
3. Staff-created reservations or native balance obligations do not establish the member’s acceptance, a deposit mandate or collection.
4. These member-facing capabilities retain SCHED-013’s saved-resource and original-funding fences.

#### ATT-001 — Member kiosk and staff check-in

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Members check in from their account, at a privacy-limited kiosk by name or phone lookup, or by scanning the member QR code or barcode shown in their app or on a key tag; front desk and assigned instructors record attendance and no-shows. Kiosks reveal no balances or other member details, and duplicate check-in never spends another credit. Staff check-in shows role-scoped alerts for an unpaid balance or failed autopay, a missing or expired waiver, an expiring pack or last credit and staff notes flagged as alerts; the kiosk shows none of them. Transport and record details are in [Studio mechanisms](docs/contract/MECHANISMS.md#att-001).

**Acceptance.**
1. Check in through member mobile, kiosk lookup, a scanned member QR code and assigned instructor doors, rescan the same code, attempt another instructor and duplicate check-in, and reconcile the class attendance roll; check in a member with an overdue balance and a last pack credit and see both alerts at the desk but none at the kiosk.
2. Only the human owner pairs a kiosk, from a signed-in owner session: pairing makes a one-time link valid 15 minutes whose secret is stored hashed; confirming it on the device mints a 32-byte random device token, stored hashed and held as an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie scoped to the documented studio door; the owner names and revokes kiosks, and a revoked kiosk is refused on its next request and at commit.
3. A kiosk is its own role with no session: it cannot open the documented studio door, the state or any command API, and a preparation-only agent credential cannot impersonate the paired device.
4. Kiosk lookup matches only active members who hold a confirmed booking in a class whose check-in is open, by name (at least 2 characters, case-insensitive, matching the start of the full name or of any word in it) or by a full 10-digit US phone (punctuation and a leading 1 ignored; the owner records the phone on the member's People card); each of at most 8 rows shows only first name, last initial, class title and time, and a member with no such booking reads exactly like no match.
5. Each member has an opaque random 128-bit check-in code (never the person id), shown as a QR code and text in their account and on the owner's printable key tag; the owner can issue a new code, which stops the old one at once.
6. Self, kiosk and scanned check-in are open from 60 minutes before a class's start instant until 30 minutes after it, [start - 60 min, start + 30 min), on the class's own studio-zone date, checked again on the database clock at commit; a scan checks the member into the earliest such booked class not yet checked in (at the desk an instructor reaches only classes they teach).
7. Staff roster check-in by the owner or the assigned instructor keeps no window. Every door consumes the seat's already reserved credit and records its channel (roster, self, kiosk, kiosk_scan, desk_scan) and kiosk id; any second check-in or rescan is refused with no credit spent. The owner and assigned instructors mark no-shows once class starts; the no-show fee is ATT-002's existing assessment (ATT-003 corrects it). Alerts are attached to each booked seat for staff: the owner sees an unpaid balance, a missing or outdated waiver (no acknowledgment of the current version), a last credit (no credits left on any unexpired pack after this booking) and a pack expiring within 7 days; an assigned instructor sees the waiver and pack alerts only, never money; members and kiosks see none. The failed-autopay alert is BILL-002/BILL-007's obligation and the flagged-staff-note alert MEM-001's (pattern 1). Kiosk device tokens are credentials: the portable exit and native archive exclude them and a restored studio pairs its kiosks again.

#### ATT-002 — Late-cancel and no-show fees

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Approved service policies automatically assess disclosed late-cancel/no-show fees or lost visits exactly once after the relevant outcome, with payment authorization, grace rules and staff waiver. A pending payment is shown separately from collected fees. A staff late cancel under SCHED-014 assesses the fee or lost visit once; a staff early cancel past the cutoff, or waiving an assessed fee, needs the fee-waiver right (STAFF-001) and a reason. Staff holding the collection right collect an assessed fee at the desk (SALE-002) or from the card on file under recorded consent (BILL-004). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Late-cancel one booking and no-show another, run the fee process twice, waive one as authorized staff and inspect one assessment per outcome with its charge status.
2. A front-desk late cancel assesses one fee, a front-desk early cancel past the cutoff assesses none with the waiver reason audited, and front desk collects the remaining fee at the desk once.
3. approved studio/service policies assess the fee from the booking's retained terms exactly once when a late cancel or no-show commits.
4. No-show grace is a whole 0–600 elapsed minutes after the class start, default zero, inclusive at the grace-end instant and fenced again on the database clock; it never postpones a late-cancel cutoff.
5. A later policy change cannot shorten a booking's grace.
6. The native owner acts for front desk and holds collection and fee-waiver rights until STAFF-001, which inherits the grants, denial and revocation cases before shipping.
7. Desk collection records an exact positive external receipt linked to the assessed fee and booking, with a globally unique receipt reference; no processor is contacted or payment authorization claimed. One receipt collects one fee once. A reversed fee cannot be collected, and an already-covered member balance refuses further collection. Unallocated historical receipts remain member-balance payments rather than invented fee-specific receipts. Pending assessments, collected fees with receipt references, and waived fees with reasons are separate on the owner/member packs doors and the member profile. Waiving an assessed fee requires a reason, appends one reversal and preserves attendance and spent-credit outcomes; waiving a collected fee leaves member credit without sending a refund. ATT-003 retains its separate attendance-correction and recorded external-refund behavior. Each staff collection/waiver retains one canonical actor/member/right/channel/before/after audit, and replay never repeats the effect. Portable exit and native archives retain the charge, linked receipt, reversal and audit relationships. BILL-004 carries recorded card-on-file consent and authorization, SALE-002 carries processor/POS desk collection and BILL-008 carries processor refunds before those capabilities ship; SCHED-002 and PLAN-001/002 inherit their fee policies before shipping.

#### ATT-003 — Attendance corrections and credit overrides

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Authorized staff correct an erroneous no-show or late cancellation, restore eligible credits and reverse or refund the fee with a reason. Corrections retain the original attendance and action history. instructors, members and preparation-only agent grants are refused with no writes. An assigned instructor or the owner marks a confirmed booking that was not checked in as a no-show only once its class has started (checked again on the database clock at commit); the no-show keeps the spent credit and adds the no-show fee in the booking's cancellation terms once. A no-show is corrected to excused (the spent credit returns to the booking's own pack) or to attended (the credit stays spent and attendance is recorded at the class start); a late cancel is corrected to an early cancel (a kept credit returns). A credit is eligible to restore only while its own pack is unexpired; otherwise the correction is refused until the owner extends the pack. Every unreversed late-cancel or no-show fee on the booking is reversed by one negative charge naming the fee it reverses. A refund of that fee is recorded only when the member's balance after the reversal shows the studio owes at least the fee, and is shown as paid back outside Strap, never as a processor refund; processor refunds belong to BILL-008. A correction needs a reason, applies once (a corrected booking has nothing left to correct), and is appended to the booking's correction history with the before state, while the original no-show or late cancel stays on the record. Future doors inherit this before they ship: STAFF-001 (the correction right for managers), ATT-002 (collected fees and processor charges), BILL-008 (processor refunds) and SEC-002 (the staff-made audit entry).

**Acceptance.**
1. Mark a paid no-show incorrectly, correct attendance as a permitted manager, approve the refund and inspect one restored credit and one fee reversal; an instructor without that right is refused.

### Billing and money

#### BILL-001 — Studio-owned Stripe connection

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Owners connect their own Stripe merchant account, confirm account identity and readiness, restrict credential access and rotate or disconnect it without losing payment history. Every charge is tied to that studio account. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Connect a Stripe test account, complete readiness, purchase a plan, rotate the connection and inspect the same merchant and retained transaction history; foreign-account webhooks cannot mutate it.

#### BILL-002 — Card checkout and recurring autopay

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Member checkout collects USD card payments, Apple Pay and Google Pay wallet payments and recurring authorization through Stripe-hosted secure fields, supports required payment authentication and starts only the accepted plan. A staff-assisted sale on a studio device (BILL-014) uses the same Stripe-hosted fields or Stripe Terminal, never a staff-typed card number. For a phone sale with no card present (SCHED-010, SALE-002) staff send the member a single-use Stripe-hosted payment link by SMS or email for the exact cart; the booking or sale completes once only when the member pays through the link, and an unpaid link expires after the studio's set time leaving no charge, sale or booking behind. Renewals use stable invoice and attempt identities. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. ATT-001 obligation before this capability ships: a failed autopay raises a check-in alert on the member's booked seats for the owner, front desk and manager, never for instructors, members or the kiosk. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Buy a monthly plan with authentication-required and declined Stripe test cards and with Apple Pay and Google Pay test wallets, complete authentication, advance one renewal and replay requests; one charge per successful invoice appears in Stripe and Strap.
2. Send a pay-by-link for a phoned-in ten-pack by SMS: paying it completes the sale once even when the link is opened twice, and a second link left unpaid expires with nothing charged or sold.
3. PLAN-010 obligation before new guest/payer doors ship: preserve guest-owned host consent, payer-owned charge authorization, studio-wide per-guest limits and the separate once-only allocation/charge ledger; STAFF-001 asserts denial and revocation at each staff door.

#### BILL-003 — ACH verification mandates and settlement

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Members authorize Stripe ACH Direct Debit with supported bank verification and a retained mandate, from their own account or by completing the hosted ACH form themselves on a studio device during a staff sale (BILL-014). Pending, succeeded, failed, returned and disputed debits remain distinct; studio grace policy controls access while pending, and a return updates the debt and entitlement state. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Authorize a Stripe ACH test bank, collect a renewal, replay pending/success/return events out of order and verify the correct open balance, mandate evidence and access without calling pending money settled.

#### BILL-004 — Stored payment methods and self-service

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Members securely add, replace, remove and select default card or bank methods through processor-hosted flows. During a staff sale (BILL-014) the member saves a method by presenting a card to a Stripe Terminal reader or Tap to Pay, or by completing a Stripe-hosted card or ACH form on the studio device. From the member profile at any time, not only during a sale, staff holding the collection right can start the same device-side save so the member taps a new card on a Stripe Terminal reader or Tap to Pay, or completes the hosted form on the studio device, and makes it the default autopay method. Staff see masked details and expiry only and never see or type raw card or account numbers; reminders lead to the member's own authenticated update flow. When saving a method the member can record consent to staff-initiated charges of it for in-person and phone purchases (SALE-002), and can withdraw that consent in self-service. Staff holding the collection right also remove a stored method or change the default on the member's request; removing the only method backing an active autopay shows the BILL-013 missing-method warning until a replacement is saved. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Replace an expiring card and verify an ACH account from self-service; the next invoice uses the selected default, another household member without payer rights cannot change it and logs contain no raw details.
2. After a decline, the member taps a new card at the desk from their profile outside any sale; it becomes the default and the retried invoice is charged to it once.
3. Staff then remove the member's old card at their request; with no other method on an autopay member the BILL-013 view shows the missing-method warning until a new default is saved, and each change is audited as staff-made.
4. APP-003 obligation before this capability ships: members replace their own card and manage their stored payment methods from their account; assert it at 390 pixels and desktop through the member's own door, with the owner seeing the same state and another member none of it, and through its STAFF-006 staff door.
5. PLAN-010 obligation before new guest/payer doors ship: preserve guest-owned host consent, payer-owned charge authorization, studio-wide per-guest limits and the separate once-only allocation/charge ledger; STAFF-001 asserts denial and revocation at each staff door.
6. STAFF-001 collection-right obligation: a currently authorized scoped staff actor may start the member’s payment-method replacement, but the member personally supplies and authorizes the method through the protected device or provider door.
7. Prove permitted, denied and immediately revoked staff access, masked resulting evidence and absence of raw payment details. A staff grant never substitutes for the member’s financial authorization.

#### BILL-005 — Scheduled invoice and renewal engine

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006

**Requirement.** Approved membership terms, whether bought at member checkout or sold by staff (BILL-014), automatically generate invoices, grant the correct period entitlements and collect dues on the studio-local billing date, once per period, including annual renewals, trial ends, scheduled cancellations and staff-made freezes, early freeze ends, terminations and canceled terminations (PLAN-012). Owners and finance see and control upcoming collections under BILL-013, and the engine honors an early collection, desk settlement, skip or postponement made there without charging a period twice or dropping it. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run renewal across restart and duplicate scheduler invocation, including a trial and canceled plan; each due period gets one invoice, the agreed access and no canceled-plan charge.
2. the native foundation proposes an exact member-accepted studio-invoice agreement on an existing pack: one invoice, renewed entitlement and balance charge per current due period, with no stored method or provider debit.
3. The consent text and acknowledgment state the amount, interval, first due date, that invoices continue until stopped, and a one-step stop link.
4. A reminder goes 15 to 45 days before each renewal of 12 months or more, and yearly for every agreement.
5. A member stops without a reason, effective at once; access runs to the term's end.
6. A period already ended when swept goes to owner review.
7. The agreement authorizes studio-balance debt only; BILL-002, BILL-003 and BILL-004 obtain their own method authorization, with ACH mandates kept 2 years after revocation.
8. Consent evidence (terms, text shown, actor, time, channel, stop) is kept for 3 years or 1 year after the agreement ends, whichever is longer. It survives erasure (restricted) and travels in archive and exit. A restore needs fresh acceptance. An owner reconnects a live origin change, keeping acceptances and notifying members. Current reminders target 15–30 days before the anniversary or qualifying renewal within the product's 15–45-day window; they create no invoice. Sweep never creates a backlog of ended periods. Calendar-month billing retains its agreed studio-local time and billing day, using the last day in a short month. Stop keeps existing debt and makes no refund. Native archives exclude the installation billing epoch, so even a same-origin restore needs a fresh owner proposal and member acceptance; a live origin reconnect instead requires the surviving epoch and preserves only its existing accepted agreements. PLAN-005 changes under an active agreement are currently refused; exact recurring changes remain owed under BILL-005. The reminder and 3-year/1-year retention rules are product policy adopted from ct, not a claim that every native invoice agreement is legally covered. Current primary statutory text for that retention rule and statutory disability/relocation exceptions has not been verified; PLAN-007/PLAN-012 retain the cancellation scope caveat. Provider collection, ACH authorization, trials, full PLAN-012 provider schedule/final-charge completion, and BILL-013 controls remain their full target obligations.

#### BILL-006 — Proration and adjustments

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Joining mid-cycle, a staff-sold membership with a future start date (BILL-014), upgrades, downgrades and freezes produce a reviewable USD charge/credit calculation with effective dates and rounding; confirmed previews are exactly what gets invoiced and collected. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Join mid-month, upgrade and freeze on specified dates, approve each calculation, compare invoice lines with previews and refuse a changed-plan stale preview.
2. the plan is the native finite pack under PLAN-005 and PLAN-006.
3. The first-term purchase is retained separately from later dated plan-change and freeze-fee lines; every line is an exact studio-balance charge or credit, not a payment.
4. A later plan change uses the current finite-term price and its remaining UTC seconds, rounded half-up; it never rewrites the original purchase.
5. Future-start staff memberships in BILL-014 and provider collection in BILL-002 and BILL-014 inherit exact accepted calculations, itemized reconciliation and stale-preview refusal before those doors ship.

#### BILL-007 — Failed-payment dunning and recovery

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Owners configure failure-specific retry, grace, escalation and suspension policies; members receive a secure payment-update link, can pay the outstanding invoice and regain the correct entitlement once. ACH-return restrictions and hard declines do not get blind retries. Staff holding the collection right act on a past-due invoice for the member: retry it now on the stored method, take payment at the desk through SALE-002, or send a BILL-002 payment link; each settles the invoice once and restores access once. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. ATT-001 obligation before this capability ships: a failed autopay raises a check-in alert on the member's booked seats for the owner, front desk and manager, never for instructors, members or the kiosk. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Fail a monthly card renewal, deliver the recovery message, update the method, collect once and inspect restored access; simulate an ACH return and a hard decline and confirm the configured separate handling.
2. Front desk retries a second past-due invoice on the member's updated card and settles a third with a cash payment at the desk; each collects once, restores access once and is audited as staff-made.

#### BILL-008 — Refunds credit notes and disputes

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Permitted humans approve full or partial refunds and fee waivers with reason, see processor eligibility and status, and reconcile credits and member access. Stripe dispute notices preserve evidence deadlines and outcome; agent refunds and write-offs carry money, adding destructive when irreversible, and the company policy decides them. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. SCHED-008 obligation before this capability ships: a closure or class cancellation that affects a paid drop-in, card-paid booking or fee offers the approved refund in the same impact preview and records it once.

**Acceptance.**
1. Refund part of a pack purchase, replay the provider event, fail a refund, and receive a test dispute; the ledger shows each state and amount, remaining credits follow approved policy and a preparation-only key cannot record a person's approval.
2. studio refund foundation: cooling-off defaults to every receipt-backed payment allocated to the agreement, including initiation and other fees, with unpaid invoices/financing voided and only governing-row deductions explicitly enabled by the owner.
3. Death, disability, relocation and other approved early terminations retain the unused prepaid calculation from actual external receipts allocated to that pack's charges.
4. The unused-time calculation excludes confirmed frozen access time and retains its payer allocation and calculation.
5. Unallocated receipts show needs-allocation and prevent settlement.
6. Owners record external settlement by payer, amount, date and reference, capped at that payer's refund due; replay reuses the same settlement and reusing a reference with changed details is refused.
7. No funds move automatically; provider refund, credit note, spendable account credit and dispute completion remain separate obligations.

#### BILL-009 — Invoices receipts and balances

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Members and payers receive itemized USD invoices and receipts with studio identity, tax, discounts, fees, tender, status and balance. Staff distinguish invoiced, externally recorded, pending, collected, refunded and disputed amounts. Staff holding the sale right email, text or print a member's invoice or receipt from the profile on the member's request, to the member's own contact details. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Buy a pack, pay partly by an external receipt and partly by Stripe, refund a line and download the member invoice and receipts; totals and each settlement state agree.
2. Front desk emails a member a past receipt from the profile; it reaches only the member's address on file and the resend is audited as staff-made.
3. APP-003 obligation before this capability ships: members read and download their own invoices and receipts from their account; assert it at 390 pixels and desktop through the member's own door, with the owner seeing the same state and another member none of it, and through its STAFF-006 staff door.

#### BILL-010 — Payment event reconciliation

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Signed Stripe webhooks are validated against the connected account and processed idempotently with event history, ordering protection and a visible exception queue. Owners can reconcile missing events against Stripe without inventing settlement. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Replay, reorder, omit and forge invoice/payment webhooks, run owner reconciliation and compare Stripe objects with Strap's invoice, access and balance; no duplicate charge or lost exception appears.

#### BILL-011 — Payouts fees and deposit matching

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Finance reconciles gross collections, refunds, disputes, processor fees and net payouts to the merchant's bank deposits by Stripe payout and transaction references, with pending and unmatched items retained. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Collect by card and ACH, refund a portion, settle a payout and download reconciliation; gross less refunds and fees equals the net payout and unmatched deposits remain visible.

#### BILL-012 — US sales tax and product taxability

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001
