**Requirement.** Owners configure service and retail tax treatment, registrations and exemptions with a US tax provider such as Stripe Tax, preserve calculation evidence and reverse the appropriate tax on refunds. Filing remains with the studio and its tax service. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Sell a taxable retail item and a differently classified class in a Stripe Tax test account, issue a partial refund and compare invoice tax and reversal with provider responses and filing export.

#### BILL-013 — Upcoming autopay schedule and collection control

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Owners and finance staff open a scheduled-collections view listing every future autopay invoice and attempt by studio-local date with the member, payer, plan, masked default payment method with an expiring or missing-method warning, amount including tax and discounts, and status (scheduled, collected, declined, suspended, skipped, postponed or canceled). Filters cover date range, location, plan, status and auto-renewing plans only, and daily and period totals forecast expected collections. Staff holding the collection right (STAFF-001) can collect a scheduled invoice now, settle it with a recorded external receipt (PAY-001) when the member paid at the desk, skip it, or postpone it to a new date, each with a reason. They can also select several scheduled invoices and collect, skip or postpone them in one bulk action; each selected invoice is previewed, processed under its own identity and audited separately, and one decline or failure neither stops nor repeats the others. Every action previews its balance and access effect, is audited (SEC-002) and leaves the renewal engine (BILL-005) charging each period exactly once or not at all, never twice and never dropping a postponed period. Autopays moved by a staff or member freeze show as scheduled after the freeze, and autopays removed by a scheduled termination (PLAN-012) show as canceled with the termination date until the termination is canceled, when they return once. Expiring-method warnings link to the member's own update flow (BILL-004), and the view exports to CSV and XLSX through RPT-006 with the same totals. Agent collection, skip and postponement calls carry money, with the company policy deciding each call. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Schedule renewals for five members over the next seven days, one on a card that expires before its date and one by ACH; the view lists exactly those five, with amounts equal to the invoices BILL-005 later creates, and flags the expiring card.
2. Collect one early, postpone one with a reason and settle one as paid at the desk with an external receipt; run the scheduler on each date, including a duplicate invocation, and see exactly one collection per period, the postponed charge on its new date, no processor charge for the desk-paid period and all three actions in the audit.
3. Select two further scheduled invoices and postpone them together, then bulk-collect two more where one card declines; both postponed invoices move and are audited individually, the good card is charged once and the declined invoice enters dunning (BILL-007) without a repeat attempt from the bulk action.
4. A preparation-only agent key's postponement is refused.
5. The seven-day forecast equals the CSV export total, and collected, desk-paid and postponed amounts reconcile to it.

#### BILL-014 — Staff-sold memberships with stored autopay methods

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Staff holding the membership-sale right (STAFF-001) sell any PLAN-001 membership or intro-to-member conversion to a member or walk-in from the staff web app or a staff phone or tablet (APP-004). They choose a start date (today or a later date, with the first charge on that date or today), apply discounts and price adjustments permitted by catalog rules and SALE-001, and see the first-period amount from BILL-006 proration before confirming. The member saves the recurring payment method themselves: by presenting a card to a Stripe Terminal reader or Tap to Pay with the card saved for later off-session payments, or by completing a Stripe-hosted card or ACH form on the studio device (BILL-002, BILL-003, BILL-004). Staff never see or type raw card or account numbers. Recurring-payment authorization, and the ACH mandate where applicable, are recorded only from the member's own action. The member then reviews, accepts and signs the membership agreement on the device in the locked signing mode (MEM-005), and the signed agreement with version, signer, device and selling staff member is stored on the profile. Only when payment-method authorization and signature are both complete does the sale activate the membership, issue the first invoice for exactly the previewed amount and start BILL-005 renewals from the saved method. An abandoned or declined sale leaves no active membership, stored method or charge. The sale is attributed to the selling staff member for commission (STAFF-005) and audited (SEC-002); an agent sale carries money and commitment under the company policy; an agent cannot record the member's own authorization. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. As front desk on a 390-pixel staff view, convert an intro member to a monthly autopay plan starting on the 1st with a permitted 10% discount; tap a Stripe Terminal test card and have the member sign the agreement on the device, then repeat for a second member who completes the hosted ACH form on the device.
2. Each profile shows the plan, the signed agreement and the masked default method, and each first invoice equals the previewed prorated amount.
3. Advancing to the 1st collects exactly one renewal from each saved method.
4. The commission report attributes both sales to the selling staff member.
5. A front-desk user without the membership-sale right is refused with no writes, an abandoned sale leaves no membership, method or charge, activation with a preparation-only agent grant is refused, and no raw card or account data appears in logs or exports.
6. PLAN-010 obligation before new guest/payer doors ship: preserve guest-owned host consent, payer-owned charge authorization, studio-wide per-guest limits and the separate once-only allocation/charge ledger; STAFF-001 asserts denial and revocation at each staff door.
7. An MFA-cleared native human owner may prepare an additional document only against an existing member-accepted invoice membership. Preparation retains the original agreement and its hash, exact additional text and version, document hash, preparing actor and adult-eligibility evidence reference. The member personally confirms adulthood, signs the exact document and separately confirms the exact recurring-payment permission, including USD amount, interval, first due date, original service period and immediate revocation path. Owner-recorded eligibility and personal confirmation are evidence of the adopted policy, not independent verification of identity, age or legal capacity. Known recorded dependents are refused; guardian, payer, household and staff status confer no representative financial signing authority. MEM-005 retains its separate personal and currently authorized guardian waiver journeys. Only the member authorizes the method through hosted TEST setup; completion requires a completed setup session, a succeeded off-session SetupIntent and matching agreement and mandate provenance before masked method evidence is retained. A return navigation, signature, permission or saved method alone never establishes collection. Current authority, accepted terms, connection authority and revocation must be rechecked before provider execution and after its response. Personal revocation prevents further use of this payment permission on the next request, preserves historical evidence and leaves original debt and service rights governed by the original agreement. This foundation performs no sale activation or automatic collection; the full staff sale, activation and renewal target remains required. The owner preparation default does not replace STAFF-001 and STAFF-006 staff rights or paired doors. MOVE-011 and ARCHIVE-001 retain portable evidence and installation-local execution authority; SEC-002 and SEC-003 retain complete audit, retention and erasure duties. This policy ruling accepts no completion, legal conclusion, production readiness or missing acceptance proof. STAFF-001 membership-sale obligation: prove the original scoped staff membership sale, separate membership-sale right, member-owned agreement and recurring-payment authorization, conditional activation and denied and revoked staff cases. Native recurring-invoice preparation supplies no substitute completion.

#### BILL-015 — Member account credit balance

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Staff holding the refund right (STAFF-001) add spendable account credit to a member from an overpayment, a goodwill credit with a reason, a BILL-008 credit note or a MOVE-008 imported opening balance, and remove it with a reason. The USD balance and its dated history appear on the member profile, invoices and receipts (BILL-009) and in member self-service (APP-003). Credit is applied at the next member checkout, POS sale (SALE-002) or autopay invoice (BILL-005) by the member's choice or the studio's auto-apply policy, partially when it does not cover the total, with only the remainder charged to the stored method. A refund of a credit-paid purchase returns that portion to credit unless a permitted human refunds it to the original tender. A liability report lists outstanding credit by member and reconciles with the ledger and RPT-006 exports; every change is audited (SEC-002), and agent issue, removal and application of credit carry money under the company policy. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Issue a $25 goodwill credit with a reason and a $15 BILL-008 credit note to one member; profile and app show $40.
2. Buy a $30 drop-in in the app applying credit, leaving $10; the next $120 autopay invoice applies the $10 and charges $110 to the card once.
3. Refund the drop-in and see $30 returned to credit.
4. Import a member with a $50 MOVE-008 opening balance and see it as spendable credit.
5. The liability report equals the sum of member balances and the CSV export, every change is in the audit, and a preparation-only agent key's credit issue is refused.

### Sales and member follow-up

#### SALE-001 — Gift cards and promotional pricing

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Studios sell and redeem gift cards, maintain their remaining liability, and apply eligible discount codes with dates, uses and stacking rules; partial redemption and refunds retain the original value trail. Staff holding the sale right sell and redeem gift cards, look up a balance and apply discount codes at POS (SALE-002); adjusting a gift card balance needs the refund right and a reason. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Buy a $100 gift card, redeem $40 with an eligible code, attempt a disallowed second use and inspect the remaining $60 liability and refund treatment.
2. Front desk redeems the remaining $60 at POS once, and a front-desk balance adjustment without the refund right is refused.
3. Semantics under the ct approval of 23 October 2026: the native owner controls recipient-bound gift custody.
4. Explicit sale and refund grants name the permitted recipients and locations; second-factor-protected front desk sees only those balances and native gift sales, without funding receipts or unrelated gifts.
5. The sale grant permits native gift issue and redemption with eligible codes; a balance adjustment or original-funding refund requires the separate refund grant and a reason.
6. Grants are checked again before replay; revocation refuses the next request without writes.
7. Gift redemption is native desk fulfillment; connected readers and provider payment collection remain SALE-002. Cards are assigned to a named recipient and have no expiration or fees; no bearer authority or automatic payout exists. Funding is an original-tender receipt and a liability rather than earned retail revenue. Eligible dated fixed-amount promotions enforce total and per-member use limits, exact SKU eligibility and explicit stacking rules; returns never reset their uses. Partial redemption is once-only and noncash, the sale preserves its discount and original-value allocation, and a purchase return restores the same card. An explicit authorized funding refund is bounded by both available balance and original funding and returns to its original tender. Every adjustment needs a reason and immutable before and after amounts. STAFF-001 inherits the named sale and refund grants, scoped denial and immediate revocation guarantees for wider roles; future member self-service under APP-001, APP-002 and APP-003 inherits recipient-owned authority, the paired STAFF-006 door and the same value trail. No new member-role command is introduced.

#### SALE-002 — Retail point of sale

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Front desk sells class products, auto-renewing memberships with a stored autopay method (BILL-014) and retail in one cart using Stripe Terminal for supported US card-present payments, including readers and Tap to Pay on iPhone and Android phones, card checkout, cash posted to the open drawer session (SALE-004) or externally recorded tenders; sales are attributed to the selling staff member and tips to the serving staff member for STAFF-005; exchanges, returns, tax and emailed receipts update money and stock together. Staff can also charge a member's stored default card or bank method for a drop-in, pack, retail item or outstanding balance sold in person or by phone, only when the member's recorded consent to staff-initiated charges (BILL-004) covers it; the member receives the receipt and the charge is audited (SEC-002). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Sell a mat and drop-in at a connected Stripe Terminal test reader, take a Tap to Pay test payment with a tip on a private session, split an allowed tender, return the mat and reconcile inventory, tax, receipt and payment without double fulfillment.
2. Charge a phoned-in member's card on file for a ten-pack under recorded consent and see one charge, one pack and an emailed receipt; a member without that consent is refused the card-on-file charge and offered a BILL-002 payment link.

#### SALE-003 — Inventory and purchase controls

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Staff manage SKU variants, barcode lookup, stock counts, suppliers, receiving, low-stock alerts and location transfers. Refunds and damaged returns explicitly choose restock treatment and keep a stock movement trail.

**Acceptance.**
1. Receive two sizes of studio apparel, sell the last unit, block overselling, transfer stock between locations and record a non-restockable return; all movements reconcile.
2. Native retail the human owner acts for stock staff until STAFF-001, which inherits location grants, denial and revocation.
3. Immutable SKU variants have unique SKU and barcode values, a supplier, unit price and a low-stock threshold.
4. Receiving retains supplier purchase references; counts keep a reasoned signed difference.
5. Each transfer writes both locations together.
6. A native desk sale records its exact price, member charge, tender receipt and stock movement in one transaction, refuses insufficient stock and retains actor-scoped once-only request identities.
7. Returns are bounded by the original quantity, explicitly choose restock or a zero-quantity damaged movement, and refund the allocated original price.
8. The private inventory screen supports barcode lookup and low-stock warnings. All sources join the current revenue projection, portable exit and native archives; restored requests never repeat fulfillment. SALE-002 retains Terminal, tax, mixed-product cart and emailed receipt obligations.

#### SALE-004 — Cash drawer and end-of-day close

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001

**Requirement.** Each register at a location opens a drawer session with a counted float. Cash sales, cash refunds, paid-ins and paid-outs post to the open session with staff attribution, and the session lists other tenders beside it. At close, staff count cash by denomination; Strap compares counted with expected cash, records any over or short amount with a reason, and produces a drawer report and an end-of-day summary by tender that finance downloads and reconciles with bank deposits (BILL-011). A closed session takes no new cash, and reopening or adjusting it requires the manager right and is audited (SEC-002).

**Acceptance.**
1. Open a drawer with a $150 float, take two cash sales, one cash refund and a paid-out, then close with a count $5 short and a reason; the drawer report shows expected and counted cash and the $5 short, a further cash sale on the closed session is refused, a front-desk reopen is refused, a manager adjustment is audited, and the end-of-day tender totals equal the day's POS transactions.
2. Native drawer the human owner acts as manager and finance until STAFF-001, which inherits permitted staff cash operations, location scope, manager-only reopen and adjustment, and immediate denial or revocation.
3. Each location and named register has at most one open session.
4. Cash sales and original-tender refunds require that open session at the sale location; paid-ins and paid-outs retain reasons and actors and cannot leave negative recorded cash.
5. Other tenders may be posted beside them.
6. Closing retains denomination counts, expected cash, signed variance and a shortage or overage reason.
7. A closed session refuses cash; an owner-manager may correct its counted amount or reopen it with an immutable audit record, retaining the original close.
8. A later session prevents reopening an older one. The downloadable local-day tender summary reconciles native retail sales, gift funding and refunds by tender with their exact sources; paid cash movements affect drawer cash rather than sale totals. BILL-011 retains bank deposit matching. The native owner requires a second factor for the page, financial commands and downloads.

#### LEAD-001 — Lead capture and attribution

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001

**Requirement.** Website forms, intro checkout, manual inquiries and consented Meta lead forms create deduplicated leads with source, interested classes, contact permission and responsible staff. Existing members keep one profile when making a new inquiry.

**Acceptance.**
1. Submit the same inquiry from a website form and a Meta test lead, assign staff, purchase an intro and verify one linked member with both source records and permission evidence.

#### LEAD-002 — Follow-up pipeline and tasks

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Staff work a lead pipeline from inquiry to intro booking, first visit, offer and membership, with owner, next action, due date, conversation history and lost reason. Overdue follow-up is visible and assignable.

**Acceptance.**
1. Assign an inquiry, schedule a callback, miss its due date, complete a first visit and convert the lead; the staff queue and stage report show each transition and its next action.
2. the native owner acts for staff until STAFF-001 and assigns follow-up only to an active native owner.
3. An inquiry links an existing People record without creating or changing an identity.
4. Each open stage keeps its assigned owner, next action and exact due instant; overdue means strictly before the current instant, and the queue sorts by due time.
5. Forms enter UTC and displays use the studio time zone.
6. Stages may be skipped or reopened with an explicit next task; intro-booked requires a confirmed booking for that person and first-visit requires an authoritative attended booking, including corrected attendance.
7. Membership records an explicit owner-reported external contract reference and conversation note, labelled as an external outcome: it grants no plan, entitlement, payment authorization or consent.
8. Lost requires a reason. Membership and lost close the current task. Conversation notes record actual conversations and never claim a send. Every change preserves immutable before/after, assignment, stage, next-action, note, native actor and time; exact request replay retains one result and changed replay or a stale version refuses. The owner queue, stage report, overdue filter and linked history agree; members, instructors and preparation agents have no pipeline data or command authority. Portable exit and native restore retain records and request identities. PLAN-001 and BILL-014 inherit native plan-sale conversion integration; LEAD-001 inherits new capture doors, MSG-001 inherits delivered conversation history, RPT-005 inherits conversion attribution and STAFF-001 inherits staff assignment and permission/revocation cases before those capabilities ship.

#### LEAD-003 — Welcome and intro nurture

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-3

**Requirement.** Approved sequences deliver welcome, onboarding tasks, first-class preparation and intro-to-member offers based on real purchase and attendance events; replies, opt-outs and conversion stop or change the next step.

**Acceptance.**
1. Buy an intro, receive welcome and first-visit guidance, attend and receive an eligible membership offer; purchase membership or reply and verify obsolete sales steps stop.
2. a native owner approves immutable welcome, first-class preparation and exact finite membership terms for an existing intro offer, and explicitly reviews actual intro-sale events for in-app delivery.
3. Member-owned in-app marketing permission is required.
4. One durable enrollment per actual intro sale retains the approved sequence and issues each welcome/guidance step once, with a private owner onboarding task.
5. Only a completed, authoritative attended booking funded by that intro creates the membership offer; reservations, no-shows and future check-ins do not.
6. A member reply, permission withdrawal, sequence pause, intro expiry or native membership purchase stops the enrollment and suppresses obsolete offers after every command and scheduler pass; stopped enrollments never reopen automatically.
7. Exact acceptance grants the approved finite unlimited-visit plan for its stated eligible services at the studio location and posts its stated charge once to the member or the already accepted household payer under MEM-002.
8. It collects no payment and creates no stored-method authority, recurring invoice or automatic renewal. The staff phone/desk/inbox door uses the same quote and outcome with the membership-sale right and one canonical audit. Members see their own messages and offers; onboarding tasks and sequence administration remain owner-only. MSG-001–009 and AUTO-001 inherit additional delivery/automation, PLAN-001/BILL-014 inherit recurring or collected membership offers, and STAFF-001 inherits expanded staff grants, denial and revocation before shipping.

#### LEAD-004 — Missed visits and lapse recovery

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-3

**Requirement.** Configured attendance drop-off rules detect a member's actual missed routine, exclude freezes, holidays, cancellations and unavailable classes, and create a staff task or consented outreach with appropriate alternatives.

**Acceptance.**
1. Establish a weekly pattern, miss visits while one member is frozen and another has no eligible class, run the rule and inspect only justified tasks/messages with the booking evidence.
2. the native owner configures the minimum consecutive weekly completed visits, missed available weeks and lookback, then explicitly refreshes private recovery tasks.
3. A routine is the same service, location, weekday and wall time in the location’s time zone; future check-ins do not establish it.
4. Each elapsed expected slot requires an actually published, completed class and a valid plan or credit at that slot.
5. Agreed freezes, canceled classes and holidays, absent or ineligible classes, full classes without the member’s own booked seat, and member cancellations or excused absences are excluded.
6. An unsettled attendance record is not called a no-show.
7. The task retains the source bookings, missed opportunities, exclusions, rule revision, native actor and time, with up to five currently bookable same-service alternatives over the next 14 days or an honest no-alternative message.
8. Nothing is sent or charged. One permanent task identity covers the member, weekly routine and last attended booking; refresh suppresses a no-longer-justified open task and never reopens a suppressed or completed identity. Owner completion rechecks the evidence and retains its note and replay identity. Members, staff and agents receive no private queue. MSG-001–009 and AUTO-001 inherit consented delivery and automation before adding any send; STAFF-001 inherits any future staff access, and SUCCESS-001–003 inherit the same evidence and booking rechecks.

#### LEAD-005 — Win-back campaigns and referrals

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1, D-3

**Requirement.** Owners segment canceled or lapsed members, approve win-back audiences, offers, referral rewards, budget and cadence, and track accepted offers through renewed membership and attendance. Suppressed members cannot re-enter automatically. Staff holding the member-record right record a referral a member names at the desk or by phone, attributed to that member. The governing outcome is recorded in [RULINGS.md RL-101](RULINGS.md#rl-101-win-back-campaigns-and-referrals). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Approve a win-back offer for eligible former members, receive a referral and renew one plan; compare redemptions, reward cost and later visits and confirm an opted-out member is excluded.
2. Front desk records a phoned-in referral, attributed once to the referring member.
3. the owner previews actual expired or effective-canceled native plans, excluding current plans, freezes, marketing suppression and the configured offer cadence, then approves exact in-app invitations and finite membership terms, referral reward and shared budget.
4. Changes after preview require fresh approval.
5. Each offer retains its source plan/cancellation IDs; exact acceptance rechecks eligibility and budget atomically and grants real finite membership access with one studio-balance charge, preserving accepted household-payer responsibility.
6. No payment is collected and no membership renews automatically.
7. Opt-out permanently suppresses an existing invitation; permission renewal does not recreate it, and a later campaign needs explicit human approval and the cadence cap. A member submits the friend email they already know; members and scoped front desk receive the same receipt shape without any matching friend ID or account/activity result. Authorized member-record staff record actual desk/phone/inbox requests with provenance; only the native owner matches and verifies a friend, and only one verified referral may attribute them. Front-desk submissions use the same receipt for unknown friends and friends already attributed elsewhere, with no matching information in scoped state or member history. The front-desk grant is checked again on the next request after revocation. A verified referral can reserve one reward identity across campaigns; reservations are never recycled after cancellation. A separate owner review requires a real renewed-plan attended booking whose class has ended before posting one negative balance charge to the referrer. The referrer’s balance reveals no friend booking or class. Owner reports reconcile accepted offers, source plans, reserved and recorded reward cost and later completed visits using those plans, without claiming causality or collected payments. STAFF-001, MSG-001–009, AUTO-001, SALE-001/002 and BILL-015 inherit any wider rights, channels, automation, reward or credit capability before shipping. Private referral attribution is owner-verified, uses one durable attribution and noncash reward identity across campaigns and cancellation, and discloses none of the friend's account, purchase or visits. Current eligibility and remaining approved campaign budget are rechecked at reservation and posting; completed attendance and separate review precede once-only credit. Broader rewards, payouts and outside campaigns need fresh reviewed authority; agent equivalents carry money and external as applicable under company policy.

#### LEAD-006 — Attendance milestones and recognition

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Studio-defined visit milestones produce opted-in congratulations, rewards or staff tasks once per milestone, using corrected attendance and member privacy choices; corrections cannot repeatedly issue rewards. Semantics for studio (ct 20 October): the owner defines a positive whole-visit threshold and label; the threshold is its stable identity, with immutable definition revisions. Only the member’s own signed-in recognition choice grants consent; it starts opted out, records actor/time/source and immutable history, and cannot be set by staff or instructors. The staff door opens the ordinary member sign-in/preferences destination without a credential, delegated authority or consent, retaining one canonical member-record audit entry. Every current authoritative booking marked attended counts once, including ATT-003 corrections; booked, waiting, no-show and excused records alone count no visit. Reconciliation runs after studio commands/check-in and on the minute scheduler, and the owner may refresh it. Each consenting active member receives at most one owner-only private task per stable threshold, with the exact attendance booking IDs, rule/consent version and actor/time. No congratulations, reward, provider message or public sharing happens automatically. Members see their own choice history and visit count; instructors and agents holding only preparation grants see no preferences or private tasks. Opt-out, inactivity, a paused rule or corrected attendance below the threshold suppresses outstanding tasks; suppressed and completed identities are never reopened, including on re-opt-in, rule edits or re-crossing. Only the owner completes an eligible open task; exact request replay retains the one completion and changed replay is refused. All rule, consent, task and audit provenance survives portable exit and native restore without triggering a repeated issue. MSG-001–009 inherit any recognition delivery, AUTO-001 inherits automation, and SALE-001/002 plus LEAD-005 inherit any rewards: all must preserve recorded consent, stable once-only issuance and no public sharing without separate permission before shipping. STAFF-001 inherits named rights and denial/revocation cases for the staff launch.

**Acceptance.**
1. Reach a 50-visit milestone, correct one attendance record and replay processing; inspect one recognition or reward and no public sharing without permission.

#### LEAD-007 — Empty-place outreach

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1, D-3

**Requirement.** Owners set audiences, lead times, class preferences, incentive limits and frequency caps for filling empty seats; outreach respects the existing waitlist and rechecks capacity and eligibility before a member accepts. The governing outcome is recorded in [RULINGS.md RL-102](RULINGS.md#rl-102-empty-place-outreach). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Open two seats in a class with a waiting list, send approved offers, race three acceptances and verify no oversell, waitlist priority and spending within the owner's limit.
2. the native owner previews and approves an explicit member audience, class, maximum lead time, per-member 30-day offer cap, exact wording, per-attended-visit incentive and total campaign budget.
3. In-app marketing permission is required; an explicit comma-separated preferred-class list must include the class service.
4. The normal waitlist runs first and its held offers consume seats.
5. Invitations reserve no seat; acceptance rechecks permission, waiver, class, booking window, pack, remaining waiters, online seats and aggregate reserved incentive under the serial revision fence.
6. Changed preview facts require a fresh review.
7. Existing campaign invitations are not automatically recreated after suppression. Members see only their own offers; the owner can accept a member’s phone, desk or inbox request with the booking right and one canonical audit, using the same booking and notice outcome. Incentive reservations are not recycled after cancellation. Only a separate owner review of completed attended classes posts the once-only negative studio-balance charge; no payment, cash payout, automatic external campaign or automatic reward is performed. STAFF-001 inherits named-right grant/denial/revocation; MSG-001–009, AUTO-001, SALE-001/002 and BILL-015 inherit any new delivery, automation, reward or credit door before shipping. A reviewed in-app offer gives waitlist priority, rechecks current consent and capacity, atomically reserves its incentive budget, and posts its once-only reviewed noncash studio credit only after completed attendance. The invitation holds no seat; cancellation never recycles a reward identity. Booking, waiver, entitlement and resources are rechecked at acceptance. Outside sends use current channel authority; agent equivalents carry external and money under company policy.

#### LEAD-008 — Post-visit feedback and review requests

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-3

**Requirement.** Feedback and review requests are marketing-class messages. Only the signed-in member may enable a separate, fully recorded feedback-requests choice, initially off; recognition consent is not feedback consent. MEM-006 marketing opt-out, MSG-008 STOP or suppression, and the booking-email opt-out for email stop queued and future requests immediately. A trigger is the first attended visit or a LEAD-006 milestone using corrected attendance only: no-shows never trigger, and correcting attendance away cancels an unsent request. Send after class and outside quiet hours, at most one request per member per 30 days and one per trigger, with no reminders. In-app and email are the initial channels; SMS waits for MSG-005/MSG-008 and member reply-confirmed opt-in, and push waits for MSG-007. Every request includes a one-click link that can only turn requests off. There is no review gating: every responder sees the same review link in the same place whatever the rating, and the link is also on the request page; no threshold, hide setting or incentive is allowed. Individual ratings and comments are visible only to the owner instructors, front desk, agents and other members see none, and no response goes to Google or marketing tools. A rating of 1–2 of 5 creates exactly one private owner task in the LEAD-002 queue and sends the member nothing. The comment box asks for no health details and comments stay private. Minors receive no requests, and guardians cannot opt in for a child. Legal adequacy under FTC review rules, Google policy, TCPA or CAN-SPAM is not asserted; implementation waits for the owner’s legal sign-off.

**Acceptance.**
1. Using member-owned feedback opt-in, complete two first attended visits, send after class outside quiet hours, submit low and high ratings and verify the same review link for both and on the request page, with one private owner task only for the low rating and no message caused by that rating.
2. Assert defaults off, consent provenance, refusal of staff/guardian opt-in, no requests to minors, no-show exclusion, correction cancellation, one per trigger and per 30 days, no reminders, every immediate opt-out/suppression including queued requests, the off-only one-click link, owner-only response access and no external response disclosure.
3. SMS and push require their receiver clauses; legal adequacy remains unasserted pending owner legal sign-off.

### Delivered communication and automation

#### MSG-001 — Two-way studio inbox

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Staff and members exchange email, SMS and in-app messages in a member-linked conversation with assignment, unread state, reply attribution, attachments and response tasks. Private staff notes never reach the member. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Reply to a class inquiry by SMS and email, assign the conversation and respond in-app; inspect one timeline, delivery outcomes and no leaked internal note.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 cancel/move/restore and waitlist controls from a member conversation with the same preview, audit and notice; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.

#### MSG-002 — Booking confirmations and changes

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Bookings, reschedules, cancellations, instructor substitutions and class closures trigger transactional confirmations with exact local time, location, instructor, policy and self-service links; duplicate events do not create duplicate notices. Staff-made bookings, cancellations, moves and restores (SCHED-010, SCHED-014) send the same notices; staff may suppress the notice for a courtesy cancel, and the suppression is recorded. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. SCHED-008 obligation before this capability ships: closure and class-cancellation notices use the studio's templates and honour each member's message preferences, keeping one in-app notice per affected booking, one message per member per closure and honest outcome labels.

**Acceptance.**
1. Book, reschedule and cancel through member doors and approve a substitution; inspect provider-received messages with correct dates and links, then replay events and see no duplicate notice.
2. A front-desk cancellation sends one notice, and a suppressed courtesy cancel sends none with the suppression audited.
3. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 exact action notices, template controls and preferences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
4. each native member or desk booking and waitlist entry, member or staff move, cancellation and restore, approved instructor/timetable change and class closure keeps the shared once-only in-app notice and configured Base email path.
5. The member-owned opt-out suppresses booking/class confirmation and reminder email while in-app notices continue; only that member can reverse it.
6. The setting discloses beforehand that sign-in/security messages, payment receipts, failed-payment notices and legally required notices continue by email.
7. MEM-006 and MSG-008 retain broader preferences.
8. Staff can launch the ordinary sign-in door but cannot choose for the member; the launch and member-session choice retain separate provenance. The owner controls plain-text opening and closing template text (up to 500 characters each); required local start/end, year, UTC offset, IANA zone, location/room, current instructor, retained cancellation policy and authenticated self-service links remain in each message. Templates apply to every existing booking/class message, including offers and grouped closure mail. This confirmation/reminder opt-out preserves existing waitlist-offer email; broader offer preferences remain MSG-008. A courtesy early cancellation suppresses both channels only with the existing audited reason. Member rescheduling shares staff eligibility, credit, cancellation-effect and pending-waitlist fences, uses online capacity and cannot override capacity or waive fees. Matching request identities reuse effects; changed identities refuse. Reservations are unique per source kind, source identity and member; initial confirmation and later offer are distinct events. Provider acceptance never claims mailbox delivery; refused, uncertain, opted-out, unavailable and restored-paused states remain explicit. Native archives and portable exit retain templates, choices, provenance, notices and outcomes without dispatch authority; after restore, the existing member-mail origin fence pauses new email until explicit owner reconnection and never replays old mail. Done covers current class and finite-pack capabilities. Before shipping, SCHED-002/005/009/012/013 inherit their record kinds and reschedule doors, STAFF-001 inherits named booking/member-record rights-denial and revocation cases, APP-001/002/003/004 inherit native/embedded/device doors, MSG-003 inherits reminder suppression and templates, MSG-005/006/007 inherit their delivery channels, and MEM-006/MSG-008 inherit broader preferences.

#### MSG-003 — Scheduled class reminders

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Owners configure email, SMS and push reminder timing by service and member preference. Rescheduling cancels obsolete reminders; delivery status and failure escalation remain visible. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Book a class, change its start before reminder time, run the scheduler and inspect one reminder for the new time and a staff task for a failed channel.
2. the native owner configures one studio email lead time and optional service overrides.
3. Service names are trimmed, whitespace-normalized and case-insensitive; an explicit disabled service overrides an enabled default.
4. Lead times are whole minutes from 1 through 10080 before the exact UTC start.
5. Rules have revisions and immutable owner before and after provenance; changed request replays refuse.
6. Each minute Cron reads the current published class and confirmed booking, rather than retaining a future send queue.
7. It sends when the current start is within the lead window and still in the future, skipping canceled, inactive/erased, already checked-in or attendance-marked bookings.
8. Changing class or start before the window makes the obsolete schedule ineligible. A booking, class and start identity reserves one in-app reminder and at most one configured Base email; edits to a rule do not reset that identity. The MSG-002 template and member-owned email opt-out apply; opting out preserves the in-app reminder and does not create a failed-channel task. Email outcomes remain provider acceptance, refusal, rate limit, uncertainty, unavailable configuration/address or restore pause. An unavailable, refused or uncertain channel creates one private owner follow-up task per delivery, including interrupted queued/sending outcomes; completing it requires a reason, records owner/time and never retries email or claims receipt. Native archive and portable exit keep rules, choices, reminder identities, outcomes and tasks; restoring never dispatches history, and new email follows the existing explicit owner reconnection fence. STAFF-001 inherits location and role-grant and revocation cases. The MSG-005 TEST diagnostic is not a reminder channel. Controlled real SMS reminder evidence still needs a verified sender and recipient; native push remains the named MSG-007 account and recipient-installation dependency.

#### MSG-004 — Waitlist offers and expiry

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** A vacancy promotes or offers the next eligible waiter according to studio policy, notifies them, enforces a claim deadline where acceptance is required and moves on after expiry; credits are consumed only when the seat is assigned. The studio can stop automatic promotion inside the late-cancel window, so a vacancy there sends a claim offer that requires acceptance, or promotes no one, instead of booking a waiter who would face a late-cancel penalty. Staff holding the booking right place a member on a waitlist, remove them or accept an offer on their behalf (SCHED-014). MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. the owner (the manager until STAFF-001) sets one studio waitlist policy: when a seat opens either book the next eligible waiter at once (automatic, the default) or offer it, holding the seat for that waiter for a claim window of 1 to 1440 whole minutes that never runs past class start; and, inside the late-cancel window, follow that policy, always send a claim offer, or promote no one. The late-cancel window starts at the cancel cutoff instant in the waiter's own booking terms (SCHED-006), so a waiter whose terms have no cutoff is never inside it. Eligible means an active member who holds the current waiver acknowledgment and a credit eligible for the class, taken in stable FIFO join order; an ineligible waiter is skipped and keeps their place. An open offer holds its seat in the online pool (SCHED-011), so a newcomer joins the waitlist behind it. The waiter, or the owner on their behalf (recorded as staff-made with the booking right), claims before the deadline (checked again at commit on the database clock): that assigns the seat and reserves one credit, the only debit. Declining is leaving the waitlist, which costs nothing. An unclaimed offer expires at its deadline: the waiter leaves the waitlist with no credit used and no fee, is told so in the app, and the seat is offered to or booked for the next eligible waiter. Expiry is applied every minute by the installation's Cron Trigger and by any booking change on that class. With promotion stopped, the freed seat stays open to direct booking. Each offer and each automatic promotion gives the member one in-app notice and one email through the installation's configured Base mail provider, reserved once per booking and sent after commit with its honest outcome label (accepted by the provider, refused, outcome unknown, not configured, no address); Strap never claims mailbox delivery. Staff place a member at the end of a full class's waitlist from the desk (recorded as staff-made with the channel) and remove them like any waitlist cancellation. Agent waitlist claim, placement and removal carry standard, adding external for a notice; the company policy decides them. Portability (MOVE-011): offers, notices and email outcomes travel in the exit and archive; the first member email records the installation it belongs to, and on an installation restored from another one every member email is reserved as paused (in-app notice only) until the human owner reviews and reconnects member email in Studio settings. Future doors inherit this before they ship: SCHED-014 (a chosen waitlist position with the override right, the cancellations list and staff waitlist audit), STAFF-001 (front desk's own booking right), SEC-002 (search and export of staff-made offer acceptances), MSG-002 (member templates and confirmations for staff acceptance), MSG-005 (SMS offers), MSG-007 (push offers), MSG-008 (consent and quiet hours for offer messages), APP-003 (claiming in the member app) and SCHED-002, SCHED-005, SCHED-012 and SCHED-013 (offers for their bookings).

**Acceptance.**
1. Cancel a full-class booking, deliver the next waiter's offer, let it expire and accept the following offer; verify FIFO eligibility, one seat and one credit debit.
2. With promotion stopped inside a 12-hour window, a cancellation 6 hours before start sends the first waiter a claim offer and books no one until it is accepted.

#### MSG-005 — Twilio SMS delivery and replies

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-3

**Requirement.** Studios connect a US Twilio Messaging Service with registered sending identity, receive signed inbound and status webhooks, handle failures and segment costs, and distinguish submitted, delivered and replied states. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. SCHED-008 obligation before this capability ships: a closure or class cancellation sends at most one SMS per affected member per closure to members who opted in, reserved with the email and labelled with the provider's own outcome.

**Acceptance.**
1. Send a studio SMS to a controlled real number after provider setup, reply to it and receive delivery events; reject a forged webhook and inspect cost/status without claiming submission proves receipt.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 action notices through the SMS provider and member preferences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
3. LEAD-008 obligation before SMS feedback requests ship: require MSG-008 suppression and quiet-hour enforcement, separate member-owned feedback consent and SMS opt-in confirmed by the member’s own reply; staff cannot perform that reply.
4. All feedback frequency, trigger and off-only-link rules apply.
5. Current MSG-005 TEST foundation: only a native human owner with a verified second factor can review the configured TEST account or explicitly submit a diagnostic.
6. Private TEST bindings never enter studio records or exports; both sender and destination are the magic test phone.
7. A claim commits before its one provider request, identical request replay never resubmits, changed content refuses, and rejected or uncertain outcomes are never automatically retried.
8. Resource segment counts and signed decimal price with currency remain separate from submission and delivery state; missing price stays unknown. TEST credentials generate no delivery or inbound callbacks. Separate signed synthetic callback doors verify every form field and exact URL, account and current installation connection, reject forgeries before writes, retain event history, deduplicate replays and prevent terminal status regression; inbound fixtures are not attributed as a real member reply. They never change member consent. Native archive and portable exit retain observations and provenance but exclude connection authority; a restored installation requires fresh owner review and never resends history. This foundation does not change the MSG-002 booking and reminder email choice or its waitlist-offer carve-out. Real messaging and every inherited obligation above remain owed.

#### MSG-006 — Postmark transactional email

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Owners configure and verify a Postmark sender, send studio booking and billing messages, correlate provider IDs, receive delivery/bounce/complaint events and see actionable sender or delivery failures. Existing authentication mail retains its separate configuration. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. SCHED-008 obligation before this capability ships: closure and class-cancellation email moves to the verified Postmark sender with the same once-per-member reservation, provider-outcome labels and no automatic resend.

**Acceptance.**
1. Verify a sender, deliver a booking email to a controlled inbox, reply through the supported inbound route and process bounce/complaint events; member history shows each outcome and failed sender setup cannot show sent.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 action notices through the email provider and member preferences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.

#### MSG-007 — Native push notifications

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Branded iOS and Android apps register scoped APNs and Firebase Cloud Messaging tokens, honor user permission and channel preferences, and open the correct booking or conversation from a notification. Revoked tokens stop being used. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Opt into push on each platform, receive a waitlist alert, open its booking, sign out and revoke permission; subsequent pushes cannot expose another member's data.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 action notices through push and member preferences; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
3. LEAD-008 obligation before push feedback requests ship: require this clause’s native permission and token lifecycle plus separate member-owned feedback consent, all immediate suppression, frequency and corrected-attendance fences, and the off-only one-click link.

#### MSG-008 — Consent suppression and quiet hours

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-3

**Requirement.** Marketing sequences require channel-specific permission and US quiet-hour settings. Twilio STOP and other provider suppression events block queued and future marketing sends immediately, synchronize to connected marketing tools and preserve the opt-out source; operational notices follow explicit permitted rules. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Queue a marketing sequence, receive a signed STOP webhook before dispatch, run the queue and synchronize Mailchimp; no suppressed SMS sends, the opt-out is visible and re-entry requires new permission.
2. Schedule a marketing SMS for a member whose local quiet hours are in effect and verify it is held and delivered once when the window opens.
3. LEAD-008 obligation before feedback requests ship: classify requests as marketing, honor separate member-owned feedback consent and all marketing opt-outs, STOP and suppression immediately including queued requests, send after class outside quiet hours with at most one per member per 30 days and one per corrected-attendance trigger, no reminders, and an off-only one-click link.
4. The booking-email opt-out also blocks queued and future email requests.
5. a configured inbound callback verifies the exact URL and sorted form HMAC-SHA1 before loading or writing studio records, checks the studio destination, deduplicates provider event identity and preserves source and date.
6. START alone never reverses STOP; new recorded permission plus a unique signed reply are required.
7. The native owner explicitly reviews the renewal queue; permission, source eligibility and pause are rechecked, and marketing SMS waits out quiet hours (20:00–10:00 always, in the member's recorded time zone, else the studio's).
8. Only native in-app delivery exists, once per enrollment; email and SMS stay held and marketing-tool synchronization is visibly pending. No automatic outbound action is enabled. Channel restrictions: STOP suppresses every member sharing the phone; re-entry needs fresh permission and a unique member reply; the 20:00–10:00 floor cannot be shortened; each held send must recheck suppression when dispatch becomes possible; the owner reviews the queue and no automatic external dispatch is enabled. Real SMS delivery after the quiet window and Mailchimp synchronization remain owed under MSG-005 and INT-005.

#### MSG-009 — One-off segmented announcements

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-3

**Requirement.** Owners and permissioned staff compose a one-off email or SMS from a template or blank editor, choose a saved or ad-hoc segment (membership type and status, location, attendance recency and frequency, intro clients, tags, lead stage) and preview the recipient count after consent, channel-permission and quiet-hours suppression. They send now or at a scheduled studio-local time, and links can open a specific class booking. Results show per-recipient delivered, bounced, opened, clicked, replied and unsubscribed outcomes in member history, and bookings that follow a click are attributed to the announcement; no separate marketing tool is required. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Send an email and an SMS announcement to active members at one location, a segment that includes an SMS-opted-out member and a member in quiet hours; the previewed count equals the sends, the opted-out member receives nothing, the quiet-hours SMS is held until the window opens, the scheduled send goes at its time, each recipient's outcome appears in member history and a booking from a clicked link is attributed to the announcement.

#### AUTO-001 — Controlled automation operations

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Owners enable rules with triggers, eligibility, channel, schedule, budget, stop conditions and human escalation; preview the affected audience and inspect run history. Pause, retries, restarts and changed member state cannot repeat charges, rewards or messages.

**Acceptance.**
1. Preview and enable an intro sequence and renewal rule, pause while work is queued, restart and change membership state; only currently authorized work runs, with a visible reason for every skip or exception.

### Member and staff applications

#### APP-001 — Branded iOS and Android member apps

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1 · *Register:* R-3

**Requirement.** Studios publish branded native apps with schedules, studio-managed class descriptions and instructor bios and photos, search and filters, plan purchase, spot selection, waitlists, guest bookings (SCHED-012), signed waivers, receipts, household access, account self-service and push. Release, store approval and update operations are part of delivery. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Install the branded app through iOS TestFlight and Android testing distribution, open a class description and its instructor's bio and photo, sign up, buy a plan, sign a waiver, choose a reformer, book and receive a push; repeat the core journey on both platforms.
2. APP-003 obligation before this capability ships: native member views carry the APP-003 profile, booking, waitlist, credit and balance doors with the same fences, audit and visibility; assert them on the native views and their STAFF-006 staff doors.

#### APP-002 — Embedded website booking and checkout

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-PUBLIC-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** The studio embeds a branded accessible schedule with class descriptions and instructor profiles, service picker, intro-offer checkout, guest booking (SCHED-012) and booking widget on its own website or subdomain. Secure sessions preserve the chosen class through signup, payment and waiver completion without leaking data to the host page. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Embed on an allowed studio domain, select a class signed out after reading its description and instructor profile, complete signup, waiver and checkout and return to that booking; reject an unapproved embedding origin and expired session.

#### APP-003 — Member account self-service

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Members use responsive web and native views to manage their own profile, household rights, bookings, waitlist positions, invoices, credits, payment methods and policy-permitted renewal, cancellation and freeze requests. the sign-in email is shown but stays an owner-only identity change (`person.save`), and a member request naming an email or another person is refused. The member-record staff door (`profile.save.staff`; the native owner until STAFF-001) edits the same fields with the same validation and records the desk, phone or inbox channel, and the owner's identity door (`person.save`) also records a member's name, email or mobile change. Every change writes exactly one profile change entry with the actor, role, channel, changed fields and exact before and after values; a staff-made entry is the canonical STAFF-006 audit with the member-record right. Absent fields keep their value; an emergency contact needs a name and a phone, and clearing both (with the relationship) removes it. A matching request identity returns the recorded profile and entry; a changed request under the same identity, a stale profile revision, an invalid phone, a half contact, an unchanged profile, another person's profile or an unauthorized actor is refused with no writes. Profile edits send no message. The owner and the member read the same current profile and change history; other members, instructors and the kiosk read none of it, and preparation-only agent keys read it only as the owner's studio context (AGENT-001). The emergency contact is a third party's personal data: the current contact and every change entry are retained while the member's record is, travel with the member in the native archive and the portable exit (MOVE-011), restore only with the member, and go with the member's erasure, which deletes the contact and keeps each change entry's actor, role, right, channel, time and changed field names without their values; a pre-erasure archive cannot restore them over a later erasure journal (ARCHIVE-001). Bookings, waitlist positions and offers, credits and balances keep their existing member doors (BOOK-001, BOOK-002, SCHED-006, SCHED-011, MSG-004, PLAN-003, PAY-001). Each future capability inherits this obligation with its own before-shipping acceptance: BILL-004 (card replacement and stored payment methods), PLAN-006 (freeze requests), MEM-002 (household rights), PLAN-007 (cancellation requests), PLAN-008 (renewal requests), BILL-009 (invoices), APP-001 (native views) and SEC-003 (used-member erasure of the contact and its change values). MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. Transport and record details are in [Studio mechanisms](docs/contract/MECHANISMS.md#app-003). The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. On a 390-pixel phone view and desktop update a profile, book/cancel, replace a card and request a freeze; verify the owner sees the same state and another member sees none of it.

#### APP-004 — Mobile instructor and front-desk work

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Permissioned staff use mobile rosters, member lookup, booking for members (SCHED-010) and standing series (SCHED-013), check-in, Tap to Pay sales (SALE-002), membership sales with a stored autopay method (BILL-014), contract freezes and terminations on a member's behalf (PLAN-011–012), booking cancellations, moves and restores with the late or early choice (SCHED-014) and every other staff door of STAFF-006, substitution requests, inbox and pay history; connectivity loss shows unsaved state clearly and queued attendance reconciles against current rights and booking state. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. At 390 pixels check a roster and member in, book a phoned-in member and sell a drop-in with Tap to Pay, disconnect during another check-in, reconnect after a booking change and resolve the conflict without a false saved indicator or extra credit debit; late-cancel a member's booking after the cutoff with the fee preview shown.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: SCHED-014 staff phone/tablet cancellation, move, restore and waitlist doors; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.

#### APP-005 — Accessible and resilient interaction

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-ACCESSIBILITY-001 · *Register:* R-1, R-2

**Requirement.** Booking, checkout, waivers, inbox and reports apply the Base accessibility duty to the studio journey. Failed reads, payments and saves show recoverable errors without false success; recovery keeps the member's choices and reports the actual state. Portable studio records keep their relationships, and restored billing and delivery remain disconnected until fresh owner reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Complete signup, booking, checkout, waiver, inbox and report journeys under the cited Base accessibility bar; interrupt a payment and a save, then recover with choices preserved and the real final state shown.

### Distribution and digital classes

#### CHANNEL-001 — ClassPass inventory and rosters

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001 · *Register:* R-4

**Requirement.** Studios connect approved ClassPass partner accounts, publish chosen sessions and allocation limits, and synchronize reservations, cancellations, attendance and settlement references. Direct and aggregator bookings share one capacity fence. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Complete ClassPass partner onboarding, publish selected inventory, reserve the last seat concurrently from both channels, cancel and reconcile the roster and settlement report; no duplicate seat or retail pack debit occurs.

#### CHANNEL-002 — Wellhub corporate-wellness access

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001 · *Register:* R-5

**Requirement.** Studios connect Wellhub (Gympass), map eligible services and member identities, validate visit eligibility and synchronize reservations, check-in and payout references under their partner terms. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection.

**Acceptance.**
1. Connect a Wellhub partner test or approved controlled account, book and check in an eligible participant, deny an ineligible visit, cancel another and reconcile roster and reimbursement references.

#### CHANNEL-003 — Livestream classes with Zoom

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Livestream or hybrid classes use Zoom meetings with member-specific access, timezone-aware reminders, capacity rules and cancellation/revocation. Recording publication requires instructor and participant consent policy. Staff holding the booking right book a member into a livestream class (SCHED-010) and resend that member's join link. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Book a hybrid Zoom class, receive an authenticated join link, cancel and attempt reuse; an entitled attendee joins while an unauthorized account is refused and recording stays unpublished until approved.
2. Front desk books a phoned-in member into the hybrid class and resends their join link; only that member can join with it.

#### CHANNEL-004 — On-demand video memberships

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Studios upload and organize recorded classes with instructor, duration, level, captions, release schedule and membership/one-off purchase access. Playback uses signed access through a video provider such as Mux and reports engagement without equating play to physical attendance. MOVE-011 obligation before this capability ships: its studio records and documents must join the portable exit with stable relationships and provenance; a native restore keeps its studio billing and delivery disconnected until the human owner reviews and approves reconnection. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Publish a captioned Mux-hosted class to one plan, watch on phone and desktop, revoke membership and verify access expiry; watch history appears separately from class attendance.

### Staff retail operations and reporting

#### STAFF-001 — Manager front-desk instructor and finance rights

*Tier:* Core · *From:* STAFF-001 · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Owners assign location-scoped manager, front-desk, instructor and finance roles and grant or withhold named rights per role and per staff member, including rights for prices, refunds, rosters, sensitive notes, exports, campaigns and staff pay. Front desk can sell, check in and book members into classes and appointments without viewing bank or pay records; instructors see assigned members only; finance sees financial reports without changing classes. The named rights through which staff act on a member's behalf (STAFF-006) are: the booking right (book, cancel, move and restore a member's bookings, waitlist, spot and guest bookings, standing series and makeups; SCHED-007, SCHED-009, SCHED-010, SCHED-012–014), held by owners, managers and front desk; the override right (book past a full roster or outside the booking window, place a member at a chosen waitlist position, move or restore into a full session; SCHED-010, SCHED-014), held by owners and managers and grantable to front desk; the fee-waiver right (early-cancel a booking after the cutoff or waive an assessed late-cancel or no-show fee, with a reason; SCHED-014, ATT-002), held by owners and managers and grantable to front desk; the check-in right (check members and guests in and launch or resend signing; ATT-001, MEM-005), held by owners, managers, front desk and instructors for their assigned classes; the sale right (sell drop-ins, packs, intro offers, course enrollments, video access, retail and gift cards, redeem gift cards, discount codes and account credit, and charge a card on file under recorded consent; SALE-001, SALE-002, SCHED-010, PLAN-004, PLAN-009, BILL-015), held by owners, managers and front desk; the price right (manual price adjustments and eligibility exceptions beyond catalog rules; PLAN-004, SALE-001), held by owners and managers; the membership-sale right (sell an auto-renewing membership and start its autopay; BILL-014), a separate right held by owners and managers and granted front desk; the collection right (collect early, settle, skip or postpone a scheduled autopay, retry or take payment on a past-due invoice or fee, and add, replace or remove a stored payment method or change the default; BILL-004, BILL-007, BILL-013, ATT-002), held by owners, granted finance staff and grantable to front desk; the refund right (refunds, gift card balance adjustments and issuing or removing account credit; BILL-008, BILL-015, SALE-001); the contract-change right (plan changes, course withdrawals, freezes, early freeze ends, terminations and canceled terminations; PLAN-005, PLAN-009, PLAN-011–012), a separate right held by owners and managers and grantable to front desk; the member-record right (create profiles and send invitations, and edit contact, emergency, household, intake, preference and referral records with provenance; MEM-001, MEM-002, MEM-004, MEM-006, LEAD-005), held by owners, managers and front desk, with restricted intake answers also needing the sensitive-notes right; and the privacy right (fulfill a member's data access or erasure request; SEC-003), held by owners only. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Invite each role at two locations and exercise every permitted workflow plus direct unauthorized API/download attempts, including a full-class booking by front desk with and without the override right, a desk membership sale with and without the membership-sale right, a staff termination with and without the contract-change right, a booking cancellation with and without the booking right and a late-cancel waiver with and without the fee-waiver right; grant one front-desk user the collection right and replace a member's card, then revoke a scope and verify the next request loses it.
2. SCHED-014 obligation before this capability ships: all SCHED-014 acceptance rights-denial cases for booking, capacity/position override and fee-waiver grants and revocation; assert the inherited behavior through its public doors.
3. SCHED-005 obligation before this capability ships: grant, deny and revoke the booking right for recurring class and appointment reservations and future-series cancellation; prove the next request loses authority, preserves every record and sends no notice after denial/revocation through HTTP and the 390-pixel staff door.
4. LEAD-008 obligation before feedback-response staff access ships: individual ratings and comments and the one private LEAD-002 low-rating task remain owner-only until a named right explicitly permits access; instructors, front desk, preparation-only agent grants and other members receive none.
5. Staff cannot set the member-owned feedback opt-in.
6. PLAN-010 obligation before new guest/payer doors ship: preserve guest-owned host consent, payer-owned charge authorization, studio-wide per-guest limits and the separate once-only allocation/charge ledger; STAFF-001 asserts denial and revocation at each staff door.
7. SCHED-009 obligation before its new door ships: preserve verified/revocable makeup authority, participant-owned adult delegation acceptance, the retained original absence/used visit, explicit credit expiry and one original-linked allocation; no erasure, import or staff grant may create a second makeup credit or expose unrelated child records.

#### STAFF-007 — Scoped signing launch authority

*Tier:* Core · *From:* STAFF-001 · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Staff launch and resend a participant's personal signing step with separate member-record and check-in scope; starting that act never grants completed-evidence access or personal signing authority.

**Acceptance.**
1.
