7. CSV uses JSON-valued data cells, with before/after objects encoded as JSON text; erased profile values remain null under the existing unused-identity policy. The HTTP doors are read-only and keep existing owner-only authority. This does not claim complete studio auditing or change retention policy. MEM-005 obligation before complete signing audit ships: preserve separately attributable launch, resend, personal completion, authority change and revocation events, with actor, participant, role, named right, channel, time, document identity, device context, authority reference and revision, relevant before and after facts and once-only event identity. Personal-copy requests and delivery outcomes remain distinguishable from signature completion. Credentials stay outside audit records and document content remains in restricted evidence storage.

#### SEC-003 — Member erasure and retention controls

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-DATA-006, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** Owners fulfill access and deletion requests for active, used and imported members, cancel or retain financial obligations explicitly, remove unnecessary notes/documents/provider tokens and preserve legally required records with stated reason and retention date. Erasure receipts and journals fence old backups without pretending to erase external copies. Owners holding the privacy right record a request a member makes in person, by phone or by email, verify the member's identity and fulfill it on their behalf. API-002 obligation before this capability ships: erased people are absent from both `roster:read` and boolean-only `attendance:read`, with every inherited API-002 fence retained. API-003 obligation before this capability ships: a used or imported member's erasure removes their rows from `attendance:read` outcome rows and retires their `memberRef`; assert it through the integration door. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Request a used member's export and erasure, resolve an outstanding invoice, verify redaction of notes, waivers and provider links under the chosen policy, retain justified financial evidence and refuse resurrection with an old backup and later journal.
2. Record a phoned-in access request, verify identity and deliver the export; the request and its outcome are audited, and a manager without the privacy right is refused.
3. APP-003 obligation before this capability ships: used-member erasure deletes the member's emergency contact and erases each profile change entry's values exactly as unused-identity erasure does, keeping only actor, role, right, channel, time and changed field names; assert it through the erasure door and a later-journal archive restore.
4. API-002 obligation before this capability ships: a used or imported member's erasure removes their entries from `roster:read`; assert it through the integration door.
5. SCHED-009 obligation before its new door ships: preserve verified/revocable makeup authority, participant-owned adult delegation acceptance, the retained original absence/used visit, explicit credit expiry and one original-linked allocation; no erasure, import or staff grant may create a second makeup credit or expose unrelated child records.
6. MEM-005 obligation before used signing-evidence retention or erasure ships: define justified record classes, retention periods, restricted access, holds and disposal for retained documents, affirmations and personal-copy metadata.
7. A hold records its basis, scope, actor and review or release conditions. Prove used-record handling and later-journal restore behavior without promising erasure of external recipients or offline copies.

#### SEC-004 — Multi-factor sign-in for privileged roles: owners, factors, recovery and reset

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008, BASE-SIGNIN-002 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** Owners enroll a second factor (passkey or TOTP authenticator) before reaching refunds, payouts, pay, exports, staff rights or provider connections. Recovery codes are single use, a factor reset requires another owner and is audited, and members may opt in. Strap's own factors protect Base sign-in. **Doors:** a person who must have a factor and has none is refused every gated page, form, JSON and download with 403 `mfa_enrollment_required`; ordinary work stays open. Anyone with a working factor (required or opted in) is held at every gated door with `mfa_required` until the session presents one, lasting that session. Second-factor-protected studio jobs: revenue and teaching-pay reports; every CSV, XLSX, text and exit download; staff history; pay rules and every pay view other than an instructor's own; staff rights (inviting, deactivating, reactivating, changing a role, and the owner's finance-report grant); preparation-only agent keys; integration keys; kiosk pairing; the member-email connection; factor resets. Refunds (BILL-008), payouts (BILL-011), Stripe (BILL-001), payroll and provider connections (STAFF-003–005, INT-001–009, MSG and CHANNEL connections) join before they ship. Agent, integration and kiosk keys never satisfy, perform or bypass a factor check. **Factors:** TOTP RFC 6238 (SHA-1, six digits, 30 seconds, one step either side, each step once per factor), secret sealed AES-256-GCM under a dedicated the installation factor-protection key generated at setup (never the session secret), setup refused while the installation factor-protection key is absent, factor active after one valid code. Passkeys use WebAuthn with user verification required, the installation host as RP, ES256 or RS256, single-use challenges expiring after five minutes, and any attestation accepted without claiming the maker checked. **Enroll/remove:** a first factor needs Base fresh email-link step-up; adding or removing beside a working factor needs the session's check; a person who must have a factor cannot remove their last. **Recovery:** ten codes shown once, hashed, each once in one transaction, counting only while a working factor exists; regenerating after a factor check voids the old set. Five wrong since the last right code pauses that person's checks for 15 minutes. **Reset:** only another owner, in a factor-checked session, after Base step-up and with a required reason; removes factors and recovery codes and ends all their sessions. Nobody resets their own; a sole owner recovers only with a recovery code, with no support or installation bypass. **Audit:** SEC-002 staff history records every enrollment, removal, reset (actor, subject, time, reason, factors removed), recovery-code use, regeneration and pause; never a secret, code or credential. **Archives:** factor secrets, passkeys and recovery hashes never enter a native archive or MOVE-011 exit; a restored studio sends everyone to enrollment, and a changed the installation factor-protection key does the same for authenticator apps with the reason shown. **Finance-report grant:** gives a member read of the revenue and collections report and its downloads only; it is not STAFF-001's finance role, which replaces it when STAFF-001 ships. STAFF-001's manager and finance roles reach gated doors only through SEC-005, and no right grants reset power. SEC-001, SEC-002, SEC-003, MOVE-011, API-002, API-003 and APP-001 inherit these protections before their new doors ship. Transport and record details are in [Studio mechanisms](docs/contract/MECHANISMS.md#sec-004).

**Acceptance.**
1. Enroll a passkey and a TOTP app and sign in with each; use one recovery code twice (second refused); have one owner reset another's factor and inspect the audit record.
2. Before enrollment, attempt an owner export, a teaching-pay adjustment, a staff role change and an integration-key creation (each refused with nothing written); verify a replayed TOTP code, a sixth wrong code within 15 minutes, a self-reset and an agent or integration key on a gated door are refused, factor data is absent from a native archive and the exit, and a member who opts in is asked for the factor at each new sign-in.

#### SEC-005 — Multi-factor sign-in for privileged roles: managers, finance staff and payouts

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-SIGNIN-002

**Requirement.** Managers and finance staff enroll a second factor (passkey or TOTP authenticator) before reaching refunds, payouts, pay, exports, staff rights or provider connections. Every SEC-004 rule applies unchanged to STAFF-001's manager and finance roles and to BILL-011's payout report.

**Acceptance.**
1. Invite a finance user and attempt a payout report before enrollment (refused), then enroll and open it; invite a manager and attempt an export before enrollment (refused).

#### BOUNDARY-001 — Payments tax and payroll custody

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-DATA-001

**Requirement.** Strap owns the studio billing, membership and reconciliation experience. Stripe operates card/ACH rails and holds payment data; Stripe Tax or the selected tax service calculates tax; Gusto/ADP execute approved payroll and filings; see [Envelope](https://github.com/just-every/run-envelope) for payroll; QuickBooks Online or Pantry is the general ledger. Provider setup and agreements are delivery work.

**Acceptance.**
1. Complete a studio purchase, tax calculation, pay export and ledger handoff; verify each provider reference and pending/failed outcome is visible without claiming Strap holds funds, files taxes or completed unapproved payroll.

#### BOUNDARY-002 — Studio scope and neighboring products

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-DATA-006

**Requirement.** Strap owns boutique fitness/yoga studio operations, including youth/family classes, course tuition and member retention. General sales CRM remains Orbit; general subscription billing remains Nectar; signatures beyond studio waivers remain Inkwell; general workflow automation remains Crank. Link these records through integrations instead of merging the products.

**Acceptance.**
1. From a studio member or invoice open a linked Orbit/Nectar/Inkwell record through a permitted integration and verify permissions and source ownership; a handoff never creates a second conflicting studio balance.

#### BOUNDARY-003 — Specialist instruction and health boundaries

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-DATA-001 · *Register:* R-9

**Requirement.** Studio intake and class attendance do not diagnose or replace a clinical record, school system or competition platform. Buyers needing belt/stripe curricula, workout performance or recital/costume administration use explicit links and exports to Zen Planner, Wodify or specialist dance software; studio membership, billing and bookings remain in Strap.

**Acceptance.**
1. Export a permissioned participant and enrollment as a Zen Planner member-import CSV, import it into a Zen Planner trial account and link its progress record back; restricted intake stays private and no medical, school or specialist-progress parity is asserted.

#### BOUNDARY-004 — Partner delivery and portability evidence

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001 · *Register:* R-10

**Requirement.** Native store approval, ClassPass/Wellhub commercial access, provider delivery, legal waiver evidence and payment-method transfer must be completed and demonstrated for production. The target includes that engineering and onboarding. Provider acceptance, human receipt and business outcome remain distinct, and unsupported export fields become migration exceptions.

**Acceptance.**
1. Inspect a controlled launch with store distribution, aggregator onboarding and live delivered messages; interrupt one provider and block one token transfer, then verify the unresolved states and member reauthorization path are visible.

### Agent surface

**Specialist: studio operations coordinator.** It follows members from inquiry through membership, attendance, payment recovery and retention, keeps classes staffed, and reconciles the studio's original records.

Inherits BASE-AGENT-001 to BASE-AGENT-013. The company's policy decides every call.

**How Strap computes each class.**

- **standard.** A reversible internal studio change counts affected records; drafting, internal tasks and bookings remain inside the business unless another class test applies.

- **external.** A notice, message, published offer, shared document or connector disclosure counts recipients. A current member or retained provider is known, an unrecognized recipient is new, and a public booking or video link is public; movement into already shared visibility also counts.

- **money.** A collection, refund, fee, credit, reward, price or payroll change carries exact amount and currency and the payer or payee, including a nominally internal ledger adjustment.

- **commitment.** Accepting membership terms, a binding waiver or a cutover or pay sign-off carries the agreement value and counterparty. An agent equivalent retains agent attribution rather than a person's signature.

- **accounts.** Creating an outside provider identity or paid resource records the provider; merely reading an already connected provider does not add this class.

- **gated.** The provider and required step identify hosted card entry, merchant verification or personal consent that the outside provider reserves for a person. A provider answer requiring that step adds gated to the saved call.

- **access.** Changing staff rights, household authority, integration scopes or agent grants counts affected people and whether authority widens or narrows; a booking or history link grants no authority by itself.

- **security.** Changing protective settings, factors or a provider credential records the setting. Ordinary member contact edits do not become security changes.

- **destructive.** An erasure, irreversible ledger removal or replacement restore counts irrecoverable items. Canceling a reversible booking or keeping a draft is standard.

- **sensitive.** Reading or changing regulated intake, guardian identity evidence, payment or payroll identifiers counts records and data kind; ordinary attendance counts and masked method labels alone are not regulated identifiers.

- **live.** Changing an active connector, running studio service or public delivery configuration identifies the service affected; a timetable draft changes no running service.

**Events.** `member.changed`, `household.changed`, `plan.changed`, `booking.changed`, `attendance.changed`, `agreement.changed`, `invoice.changed`, `payment.changed`, `campaign.changed`, `import.changed` and `studio.changed`, alongside `approval.pending`, `approval.decided`, `review.flagged` and `policy.adopted`. **Prompts.** `run-studio-week` covers SCHED-001, SCHED-004, BOOK-001, BOOK-002, STAFF-002 and STAFF-003; `retain-members` covers LEAD-002 through LEAD-008 and SUCCESS-001 through SUCCESS-004; `recover-payment` covers BILL-007 and SUCCESS-005; `reconcile-cutover` covers MOVE-001 through MOVE-010 and SUCCESS-007.

**Not tools, and why.**
- `DATA-001`: Transactional consistency is an installation fact; the booking and ledger tools exercise it.
- `PUBLIC-001`: The run-site, Lobby and documentation navigation are a person's interactive screens and installation facts.
- `APP-001`: Native device installation and app-store distribution are device operations and installation facts; their studio actions are covered by the corresponding tools.
- `APP-004`: Phone and tablet presentation is a device screen; the corresponding staff actions remain tools.
- `APP-005`: Accessibility and recovery are properties of a person's interactive screens; the underlying actions remain tools.
- `BOUNDARY-001`: Provider custody and payroll or ledger delivery belong to connectors; billing and payroll preparation remain studio tools.
- `BOUNDARY-002`: Cross-product record links are connector operations; each product keeps its own records.
- `BOUNDARY-003`: The specialist progress transfer belongs to the import door and connector.
- `BOUNDARY-004`: Commercial partner admission and distribution are installation facts; interruption and recovery remain the corresponding connector jobs.
- `CHANNEL-001`: This is the named connector's door; studio changes it triggers are the same registered studio actions.

**Limits.** The installation documents capacity bounds for request rate, body bytes, returned records and archive size. Business volume, recipient thresholds and provider spend are company-policy facts, not installation capacity. **Handoffs.** Listen for permitted Business identity changes, provider payment outcomes and connected calendar, aggregator and access events. Emit studio booking, attendance, payable, consent and cutover events with their original record references for the receiving specialist.

#### SURFACE-001 — Studio action registry and Core coverage

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-002, BASE-AGENT-006

**Requirement.** The studio registry covers the tool table and the stated reasons for device, connector, import and installation jobs.

**Acceptance.**
1. Compare every Core clause, including split parts, with the registered tool metadata or the stated non-tool reason; no clause is absent.
2. Run a booking, cancellation, sale, staff change and renewal follow-up through the UI and registered tools on the same studio fixture; read back the same state and audit event.
3. The studio's embedded retention and scheduling agents call these registered actions and produce the same class computation as an outside specialist.

#### SURFACE-002 — Studio business and provider classes

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-006

**Requirement.** Strap computes internal, outside, financial, binding and provider classes from current studio facts.

**Acceptance.**
1. An internal reversible booking or task carries standard with its affected record count.
2. A member message, public link and newly shared document carry external with recipient count and known, new or public counterparty as described above.
3. A sale, collection, refund, credit, fee, reward and payroll adjustment each carry money with exact amount, currency and counterparty.
4. A binding membership decision, waiver equivalent and cutover or pay sign-off carry commitment with their agreement value and counterparty.
5. Creating a provider identity or paid resource carries accounts with its provider; reuse of an existing resource alone does not.
6. A hosted card-entry, merchant-verification or personal-consent step carries gated with its actual provider and step; a provider response demanding a person adds that class.

#### SURFACE-003 — Studio authority and protective classes

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-006

**Requirement.** Authority, protection, irreversibility, regulated data and running-service changes have distinct studio facts.

**Acceptance.**
1. A staff, guardian, integration or agent-authority change carries access with people and direction; a history link alone changes no authority.
2. A protective-setting, factor or credential change carries security with the setting.
3. Erasure, irreversible ledger removal and replacement restore carry destructive with their item count; a reversible cancellation or draft does not.
4. Regulated intake, guardian identity evidence, payment or payroll identifiers carry sensitive with records and kind; masked labels and ordinary attendance counts alone do not.
5. A running connector, public delivery or service-configuration change carries live with its service; an unpublished timetable draft does not.
6. Agent arguments or imported prose claiming that a recipient is internal, a step confirmed or a record harmless do not alter these computations.

#### SURFACE-004 — Studio resources and private relationships

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-003

**Requirement.** Studio resources preserve the separate member, participant, payer and staff relationships.

**Acceptance.**
1. Read one household as its member, its payer and a staff sponsor: each receives exactly the corresponding studio view, including restrictions on unrelated participants and financial history.
2. Follow links returned from a booking, plan change, invoice and signed-document action to the resulting record and its original source relationships.
3. An imported note, member reply and uploaded waiver text remain untrusted data when supplied to the specialist.

#### SURFACE-005 — Studio sponsor and grant intersections

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-004, BASE-AGENT-005

**Requirement.** A specialist uses the sponsor's current studio role and explicitly narrowed member and location scope.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run the studio staff-role matrix through MCP with a one-location, named-member grant; the same read and mutation decisions occur in the staff door.
2. Revoke the sponsor's booking right during a saved move and retry; the booking, original funding and notice queue remain unchanged.
3. Under the all-autonomous company policy, attempts to widen the specialist's own grant or sponsor role, or change policy or Pause, fail without a studio effect.

#### SURFACE-006 — Company policy across studio jobs

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-007

**Requirement.** The same studio job carries the company's decision across outside and embedded agents.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run booking, fee adjustment, outside reminder, contract change and provider connection fixtures through every agent door under the company's selected dispositions; read their studio effects and saved facts.
2. Change one class rule while a studio action is pending; the next allowed call cites the adopted policy revision and changed inputs cannot reuse the earlier saved decision.
3. Before the company answers every studio class, the setup questions show the actual studio examples and no selected answers; unanswered classes create no studio effect.

#### SURFACE-007 — Studio acts and personal evidence

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-008

**Requirement.** Studio receipts distinguish the agent's act, the sponsor and any person's separate personal act.

**Acceptance.**
1. Execute an allowed agent contract or field decision and inspect the member history, document, activity and export: each attributes the agent acting for the sponsor.
2. After the named person approves a held company-policy action, the studio record names that person as approver and the specialist as preparer.
3. An agent cannot manufacture a member's own waiver signature, recurring-payment permission, SMS opt-in or hosted card-entry act; the permitted agent equivalent carries its own class and truthful attribution.

#### SURFACE-008 — Studio action overview and ledger trace

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-009

**Requirement.** The Agents view connects each studio effect to its class, decision and original record.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run a fixture week of booking, sale, fee waiver, message, consent and cutover jobs; the by-class counts reconcile to the complete audit set.
2. Inspect review, approval, refusal and handoff rows and follow each to its member, class, invoice or source import without exposing another sponsor's restricted data.
3. Undo a reversible reviewed studio task and reconcile the effect, review record and original audit without deleting its history.

#### SURFACE-009 — Studio events and specialist handoffs

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-010

**Requirement.** Studio events retain the identity of the change that another specialist follows.

**Acceptance.**
1. Change a booking, attendance outcome, payer responsibility and invoice in the product; the subscription, cursor feed and webhook use each change's same event identity.
2. Revoke a member or location grant before delivery and read all three channels; none exposes the restricted studio event.
3. Replay a provider-payment or aggregator event into its studio handoff and inspect exactly one resulting business effect.

#### SURFACE-010 — Studio capacity and company budgets

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-011

**Requirement.** Installation capacity and company-chosen business budgets remain separate in studio work.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run studio booking and report calls at their documented request, body and result-size bounds; capacity errors leave the saved booking and ledger unchanged.
2. Under a company policy with a recipient threshold and provider-spend cap, inspect the message that crosses each bound and the recorded decision and spend.
3. Under the company's no-cap answer, a spending studio call runs and usage records its cost; Pause stops agent work while member and staff sessions continue.

#### SURFACE-011 — Booking and billing conflict receipts

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-012

**Requirement.** Studio retries retain one business identity across allocation, charge and notification work.

**Acceptance.**
1. Retry a booking, fee, refund, reward and renewal action after losing its response; each returns the retained outcome with one allocation, ledger effect and message identity.
2. Change the expected revision after another booking consumes the last seat or credit; the stale call changes no allocation, charge or notice.
3. A long migration or report job exposes its handle only to the authorized sponsor.

#### SURFACE-012 — Studio agent documentation and prompts

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-013

**Requirement.** A stranger discovers the studio jobs and their class facts from the generated on-site agent guide.

**Acceptance.**
1. The guide lists the exact studio tool and resource names, each computed class and its fixture, and the capacity and error details the door serves.
2. From only the studio URL, discover the four specialist prompts, choose the seeded-week job and follow its source-record links without a private builder instruction.

#### SURFACE-013 — A studio week under two company policies

*Tier:* Agent surface · *From:* new · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-001, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-013 · *Register:* R-11

**Requirement.** A stranger specialist completes one seeded studio week through MCP under the company's all-autonomous and held policies.

**Acceptance.**
1. Given existing personal membership and payment permissions, schedule the week, book an eligible member, fill a cancellation, prepare instructor pay, run an allowed renewal follow-up and reconcile payment exceptions through the studio tools.
2. Under the company's all-autonomous policy for every declared class, those calls complete without a person, a policy refusal or a pending approval.
3. Under the held company policy, standard calls enter review and each other declared class waits for the named person; after that person acts in the returned browser door, each saved action completes exactly once.
4. Reconcile bookings, credits, balances, pay, notices and the by-class audit across the two runs; facts and outcomes equal the policy in force.
5. Before setup, attempt the first studio call and inspect no business effect and the setup questions. After Pause, the next specialist call has no business effect.

## Push further

### Push further

#### SUCCESS-001 — Detect explainable attendance drop-off

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** The member-success agent joins real booking/attendance patterns, membership/freeze state, available classes, cancellations and consent to explain a deviation from a member's own routine. It cites dates and uncertainty, excludes unavailable opportunities and queues outreach under owner frequency limits.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run an approved member journey through public booking/attendance doors, remove visits for one routine and freeze another member; inspect cited risk reasons, no false freeze alert and no message without permitted policy.

#### SUCCESS-002 — Offer a workable attendance plan

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-DATA-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** Using member-provided schedule preferences, travel and location choices, class eligibility, remaining credits, live capacity and actual visits, the agent offers feasible class times and a plan change or freeze request when useful. It never invents availability or diagnoses health. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Give a member a narrow available schedule and no entitlement to reformer classes; inspect only eligible available choices, accept one and verify the offered class remains feasible after a concurrent booking.
2. the member alone records dated availability intervals and chosen locations.
3. An owner, connected preparation key or explicitly scoped front-desk user prepares at most ten chronological published choices, citing class identities, current eligible packs and actual attended booking identities.
4. The entire class must fit an interval and chosen location; current credits, plan hours, freeze and cancellation state, intake, waiver, booking windows, existing overlapping bookings and online capacity are checked.
5. Offers hold no seat and send nothing.
6. No eligible choice is stated honestly, with the existing member plan-change and freeze request doors; those retain their own human approval requirements.
7. Member acceptance or the booking-right staff pair rechecks current source details and eligibility inside the ordinary booking transaction; a changed preference, changed class or filled seat refuses without partial booking or credit use. Replay preserves one booking and its ordinary confirmation. A separate owner-granted attendance booking scope and enabled member-record scope must both name the member and location on every front-desk request, including replay; second-factor protection applies and revocation is immediate. Staff can start the member sign-in step but cannot set availability. Preferences, scope revisions and source-linked offers remain portable. No health diagnosis, autonomous contract change, refund or outbound campaign is performed. STAFF-001 inherits these named booking scopes and denials for wider roles; SUCCESS-003 retains approved freeze and refund execution, and AUTO-001 and MSG clauses retain future automatic triggers and outreach obligations.

#### SUCCESS-003 — Book accepted help under company policy

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2, D-1

**Requirement.** After a member accepts a specific class offer the agent books through the ordinary capacity, waiver and entitlement rules and confirms the result. Refunds and debt waivers carry money; binding plan or contract changes carry commitment, and reversible freezes carry standard, adding money for a fee. The company policy decides each authorized agent call, using its exact saved effects. The member/staff pair follows D-1.

**Acceptance.**
1. Accept a suggested class, fill its last seat concurrently and verify a truthful conflict or single booking; request a freeze and refund and under the held company policy require the named person's approval and recheck the saved facts before execution.

#### SUCCESS-004 — Measure recovered visits and renewals

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** The agent follows accepted and declined interventions through subsequent attendance, collected renewal revenue and member feedback, separating correlation from demonstrated experiments. Owners approve cohorts, incentives and spending; small or incomplete groups stay inconclusive and members can opt out.

**Acceptance.**
1. Run an approved attendance-recovery campaign, record later visits and one failed renewal, compare observed and collected outcomes with source events and inspect an inconclusive small cohort without a fabricated success claim.

#### SUCCESS-005 — Recover billing without breaking access

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** The agent compares invoice/payment events, ACH return state and entitlement policy, explains the actual failure and prepares a secure recovery action. Owner-set retries and grace may run deterministically; refunds, waived debt and contract changes require human approval.

**Acceptance.**
1. Return an ACH debit after apparent success, ask for recovery help and inspect the provider-grounded explanation, correct debt/access and a secure update link; under a company policy that blocks money calls refuse the attempted debt waiver.

#### SUCCESS-006 — Balance demand and instructor coverage

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** The agent joins waitlists, occupancy, attendance, instructor availability and substitution history to propose additional classes, retiming or coverage. Timetable and staffing changes carry standard, adding external for member notification; the company policy decides an agent call on the exact change after existing bookings are rechecked. the connected agent reads cited class/booking IDs and counts of usable seats, reservations, held offers, waiters, attendance and no-shows, together with retained substitution history and recorded availability, then supplies an additional-class, retiming or coverage proposal and its rationale. These are observed counts, not a demand forecast or a claim of recovered visits. Availability uses active instructors, published resource allocations and recorded unavailable intervals with the existing preparation/cleanup buffers and half-open UTC bounds (SCHED-003); absence of a block is not an instructor confirmation. Exception reasons stay owner-only. A frozen recommendation keeps those facts, the exact class, reservation/credit effects and member notice text; changed availability, bookings, qualifications or provider/origin facts require a fresh preview. The ordinary classes.publish door cannot publish a recommendation. Coverage requires the studio's current service qualification and retains substitution provenance. Until STAFF-001 the native human owner acts for the manager; a preparation-only grant permits preparation, and STAFF-001 inherits the manager grant, denial and revocation cases before shipping. At approval the owner explicitly selects the shown notification: in-app plus configured email for each affected reservation, or no affected reservations for an addition/empty class. Notice suppression is not offered by this door; provider acceptance is not mailbox delivery, and unavailable or paused email is labelled honestly. Publication preserves current booking IDs, waitlist positions, reserved credits and charges; the same approval never repeats a class, audit or notice.

**Acceptance.**
1. Create overfilled and underused classes through public doors, provide instructor availability and request a recommendation; review its cited demand, change availability before approval and require a fresh preview.

#### SUCCESS-007 — Make cutover reconcile

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** The migration agent maps actual source members, bookings, plans, credits, signed-waiver evidence, schedules and processor-transfer exceptions, proposes ambiguous matches and explains discrepancies. Balance and cutover sign-off carries commitment; activating autopay carries money and commitment, with the company policy deciding an agent call. Missing source values remain unknown.

**Acceptance.**
1. Upload a real source package with duplicate names, a missing pack balance and an unsupported payment method; inspect cited proposed matches, resolve ambiguities and under the held company policy require the named owner's commitment approval before collection.

#### SUCCESS-008 — Resolve routine member inquiries

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008 · *Rules:* D-2

**Requirement.** A scoped inbox agent answers from current studio policies, member entitlements, schedules and conversation history, cites its facts and proposes allowed bookings or self-service steps. It escalates uncertainty, sensitive intake, complaints, refund requests and contract changes to people and respects all channel preferences.

**Acceptance.**
1. Ask about credits, a class time and a freeze by SMS and in-app, change the source schedule mid-conversation and verify grounded updated answers, approved booking actions and human escalation for the freeze and refund.
